# HANDOFF: Prototype 2 → native iOS port (iPhone-only workflow)

_Last updated: 2026-11-03 (Turn 61). This is a living document. See "Maintenance protocol" at the bottom._

---

## 0. TL;DR for a receiving agent

- **Goal:** Play *Prototype 2* on an iPhone with real native performance and good battery life, instead of emulating the PC version.
- **Hard constraint:** The user has **no Mac and no PC workflow for this**. Everything must be doable from an iPhone (Safari, GitHub mobile app, Files app) plus **GitHub Actions cloud runners** (including `macos-latest` for Xcode builds).
- **Key discovery (Turn 4):** The Android "port" the user has is **not a normal Android game port**. It is almost certainly a **static recompilation of the PC game's x86 code into native ARM64 C++**, wrapped in a thin Win32/D3D9/XAudio2/XInput compatibility runtime that renders through **Vulkan 1.3**. The entire game lives in one 97 MB ARM64 library, `libp2re.so`, and the Java side is trivial. See §4–5.
- **Why that matters:** The game code is already native ARM64, so "porting" means **replacing the thin Android platform layer and getting the ELF code to run on iOS**, not rewriting the game. This is far more tractable than the original plan (decompile Java, reimplement logic in Metal), which is **not viable** here.
- **Top blockers (status Turn 16: TPIDR_EL0 is non-zero on the device, see Turn 16 update; Turn 14: blocker 1 RESOLVED for static embedding, patched code runs on the phone; blocker 2 patched and window addresses proven free, runtime's own reservation not yet exercised):** (1) getting the Android ELF `.so` to execute on iOS, and (2) **a fixed 4 GB guest memory window at virtual address `0x2000000000`** that the compiled code hard-wires (see §4b, §7). The second one is a **go/no-go test that needs a probe app on the user's actual iPhone** (see §11).

- **STATUS AT TURN 56 (2026-11-03) [CONFIRMED from `p2-disasm.zip` (`disasm.txt` for the three Turn 55 ranges; llvm-objdump of the untouched ELF); reading by hand; mechanism INFERRED but strongly supported]: THE `__cxa_guard_acquire` ABORT IS OUR `syscall` SHIM (it still goes straight to Darwin), NOT FIBERS.** (1) The guard function (`__cxa_guard_acquire`, `0x5b05cdc`) identifies the calling thread with **`syscall(178)`** (`mov w0,#0xb2; bl syscall@plt`, twice: `0x5b05d34` and `0x5b05d84`); 178 is `SYS_gettid` on Linux aarch64. Flow [CONFIRMED listing]: lock global mutex `0x6039070`; read guard byte 1 (`[x19+1]`); if bit 1 (in progress) is set, load the stored thread id `[x19+4]`, call `syscall(178)`, and **if equal branch to `0x5b05df8` = the \"recursive initialization\" abort message** (return address `0x5b05e04` = the frame seen in every crash); otherwise wait on cond `0x6039098`. When the guard is free it stores the caller's `syscall(178)` result at `[x19+4]` and sets byte 1 = 2. (2) `syscall` is on the loader's `IMPORT RISK` list since Turn 28 and nothing shims it. On Darwin, `syscall(178, ...)` is NOT gettid [UNVERIFIED which Darwin call 178 is; not looked up; it is not gettid]. If it returns the same value on every thread (e.g. -1), then **any second thread that reaches a guard while another thread is still inside that static initializer sees \"stored id == my id\" and aborts as \"recursive\"**. That fits every observation: nondeterministic, timing-controlled (`imgdelay`/`pdelay` move who overlaps), two different callers, before and after state 6, good- and bad-shaped launches alike. The fiber theory of Turn 55 item 5 is NOT needed [INFERRED, replaces it]. (3) The callers are plain function-local statics in the dev's own host code: `f_10895b40` (`0x59fe280`) guard at global `0x5daf560` (call at `0x59fe62c`, return `0x59fe630`), `f_10862230` (`0x59feac0`) guard at `0x5daf570` (call at `0x59fed58`, return `0x59fed5c`); both sit in the render/decl-hook area; the second one calls `vkpddi::DebugOff(char const*)` right after taking the guard (so it is a `static bool x = DebugOff(\"...\")` style init). Both are called from more than one thread. (4) **Why the process then dies with SIGSEGV [INFERRED]:** the abort helper at `0x5b0674c..0x5b06774` calls `android_set_abort_message`, `openlog`, then **`syslog(2, fmt, x2)` with the message argument in a register** (Android variadic ABI); Darwin `syslog` reads variadic arguments from the stack, so it dereferences garbage (the per-launch garbage pointer in x0/x1/x3 of the crash) at `pc=0x18e1b48e4`. So the SIGSEGV is a SECOND bug (unshimmed `syslog`), only visible because the first one aborts. (5) **v43 PLAN (not written, needs `build-loader-v42.yml` re-uploaded):** a real `sh_syscall` shim (map Linux aarch64 numbers: 178 -> a unique per-thread id, e.g. `pthread_threadid_np`; log and return -1 with errno ENOSYS for any other number, first 20 logged with caller), a `sh_syslog`/`sh_vsyslog` that formats through the existing variadic register-file machinery (or just logs the format string and arguments to the loader log), and a log line for each use of `syscall` (number, caller). Expect the crash rate (2 of 10 with v42) to go to 0 and, **possibly, the good/bad split itself to change**, because other code may also use thread ids from `syscall`/`gettid` (check `gettid` shim returns a unique id too). After v43, re-measure good/bad with and without `imgdelay`; the delay may become unnecessary. See TURN 56 ADDENDUM.

So Q's global is guest `0x110091e0` (x22-456) and Q and the entry array are 32-bit guest pointers; my Turn 44 inference (`elf+0x5daf108`) was a mistake and the v32 DROPMON would have read garbage (it would have shown `qbad`/`rdfail`, not a false conclusion). **v33 fixes it.** (2) **The lookup helper `0x59fae80` is read [CONFIRMED listing]:** `lookup(reg x0, key w1)` has a **per-thread cache (reached through a TLS descriptor call at `adrp 0x5c70000 +0x680`) of 16,384 entries x 12 bytes {tag, key, id}**, index `((w48 ^ key) * 0x9e3779b1) >> 18`, tag = `([reg+0x34] & 0x0fffffff) | [reg+0x30]` (reg+0x34 = the counter w52, reg+0x30 = w48). A cache hit returns the cached id; on a miss it takes the lock (spin 64 times then `sched_yield`), does the hash-bucket walk (node: next +0, hash +8, key +16, value +20), and **if the key is absent it STORES id 0 into the cache with the current tag** (`stp w22,w19,[x21]; str wzr,[x21,#8]` at 0x59faff8-0x59fb004). So **a miss is cached as 0 until the counter at reg+0x34 changes**; the counter changes when the hook registers something (and it grows by thousands while the decl map holds only 20-21 entries, so the hook runs very often). (3) **Good vs bad v31d logs [CONFIRMED]:** `MAPSTATE DECLMAP lock=0` always, size 3 -> 20/21, buckets 5 -> 23 in BOTH; SHADERMAP size 1341, buckets 1597, counter 0x53e in BOTH; `LSEFLAG = 1` in both. So the map is NOT empty or locked in the bad launch (candidates A and B dead, from the logs themselves this time). Bad run: 2,478 skipped/s (59 passes/s), 32 distinct `decl 00000000` lines; good: 122 skipped/s, 1 such line. (4) **Startup ordering reproduced, n=2 pairs [CONFIRMED log order]:** good: `state 6 target 3` (line 416) -> `graphics LOW` override (422-424) -> `state 0 target 3` (426); bad: `state 6` (416) -> `state 0` (422) -> override (433-435). (5) **New candidate E [INFERRED]:** a stale cached miss: in a bad launch the engine looks up decl objects (reader, some thread) before the hook has registered them, the helper caches id 0 per thread, and the cache entry is not invalidated while the counter stays unchanged, so the same thread keeps getting 0 for that key. The cache is per thread and sits in TLS (our TLSDESC resolver); if our resolver hands a thread a block that is not zero-initialised or shared, other symptoms would follow. Not tested. DROPMON separates it: map hit (direct find) while the game still drops = stale cache. v33 WRITTEN (`build-loader-v33.yml`), not built, not run. See the TURN 45 ADDENDUM.

The real draw function (frame size `0xf10`, saves registers at `0x59e7960`, body from `0x59e7980`) has NO exported symbol; every earlier `ShadowDrawSkipped+0x...` label was just the nearest preceding symbol. (2) In that function `x26` = the draw-state snapshot: `[x26+0]` vertex shader id, `[x26+4]` pixel shader id, `[x26+8]` vertex declaration id, `[x26+0x1a4]` FVF (`-1` = \"use the current FVF\"). Logic [CONFIRMED listing]: vs and ps ids are resolved to shader objects (zero object = flag cleared); if the decl id is non-zero it calls `vkpddi::DeclElements(decl)` and continues if that returns elements; if the decl id is 0 it takes the FVF (if `-1`, `NullCurrentFVF()` = a getter of the global at `0x5daf798`); **if the decl lookup fails or the FVF is 0, it jumps to `0x59e7e60`: builds the \"(missing)\" flags from \"vs object found / ps object found\" and logs `draw left out: vs ps decl` (de-duplicated, 32 keys), returns 0 (draw not issued).** So `decl 00000000` means the game's draw had NO vertex declaration AND FVF 0 (or current FVF 0). Why the game's draw state has both zero is UNKNOWN. (3) `NullCurrentFVF` is only a getter, so the earlier remark that the layer \"has special FVF handling\" is much weaker than written in Turn 39. (4) `Present()` builds the `rtdump` path from a global `std::string` (GOT slot `0xe10`, string at +8; very probably the game folder `.../Prototype2/game`, so the file would be `.../Prototype2/rtdump` after our path remap) and elsewhere in `Present()` also does `access()` + `remove()` on a marker path (an armed-once marker; which one is UNKNOWN). **Net: the full-screen-quad theory stays a theory (no evidence the dropped draws are the world), and the dropped draws could be dropped on Android too (no Android reference).** The dev's own `rtdump` and `P2RE_VKDEBUGCLEAR` are now the best next test. v30 (same file) unchanged this turn.   **Decoded fault site [CONFIRMED words, hand decode, cross-checked against the register dump]:** `elf+0x5367168` = **`ldar w20, [x21]`** (32-bit load-acquire, word `88dffeb4`). `x21 = 0x7800000000` = guest base. Just above it: `ldr w26,[x19,#0]` (x19 = `Cpu*`, so w26 = **guest EAX**, +0 of the Cpu struct) then `orr x21,x26,#0x7800000000` (host pointer = guest address | base), `and x24,x26,#3` and `cbnz x24,elf+0x5367188` (an alignment test; x24 = 0 so the aligned fast path runs). Dump shows **x26 = 0**, so **guest EAX was 0 and the guest code did an atomic read through it: a genuine guest null dereference (read of guest address 0)** [CONFIRMED by registers: x26=0, x21=base, `fault ... is guest address 0x0`]. Also just above: `str w26,[x8]` with `x8 = 0x78097eb670` (a guest-stack address near x0 = 0x97eb610), i.e. the code **pushed EAX (0) on the guest stack** before the load [INFERRED from the store]; the lines before (`ldrb w10,[x19,#0x200]` etc.) are the lazy flag bytes at Cpu+512..523 being read. Words after the ldar: `mov w1,#1; mov x2,x21; mov w0,w20`. Four words were decoded only by pattern and are not needed for the conclusion (`fc414268` ldur d8,[x19,#20]; `11018108` add w8,w8,#0x60; `293f2ba9` stp w9,w10,[x29,#-8]; `29012be9` stp w9,w10,[sp,#8]) [UNVERIFIED].
  **Where the null probably comes from [INFERRED]:** `lr = elf+0x536710c` is 0x5c bytes before the dump window start, i.e. this guest function **called something at about elf+0x5367108 and, after it returned, reloaded EAX from the Cpu struct (`ldr w26,[x19]`)**. EAX is the x86 return register, so the most likely story is **the callee returned 0 (NULL), the caller did not check it**. The callee is either another recompiled guest function or a host/shim call (a Win32 API thunk, an allocator, a file/handle call). Not proven: instructions between 0x536710c and 0x5367128 were not dumped. The frame chain (elf offsets, innermost first) is `52465dc 524aa40 524ab54 4545474 416f740 59ce97c 3f56068 41798ac 59ce97c db6eec db8f08 59ce97c 5a90e80`, ending in garbage; `59ce97c` repeats (looks like a dispatch/fiber trampoline return, UNVERIFIED).
  **Why SIGBUS, not SIGSEGV [INFERRED]:** Darwin reports a page that exists but forbids the access as SIGBUS (`sig=10`); an unmapped address would be SIGSEGV. So **guest page 0 is mapped with no access** (the runtime's own `PROT_NONE` guard or an uncommitted page). Who set that, and whether the dev's code intentionally guards page 0, is **not known**. It does not matter for the diagnosis: a null EAX would also fault on Android unless the dev's runtime maps page 0 readable (the Android build was never observed here), so the question is "why is EAX 0 here" first.
  **Still open [UNKNOWN]:** the identity of the callee at ~`elf+0x5367108`; whether any of our shims returned 0/-1 where Android returns a valid value (candidates from the logs: `socket(2,2) -> fails` just before; the `IMPORT RISK` list; `mmap`/`mprotect`/`madvise`/`posix_memalign` results when the guest asks for memory; the new scanf shims; clock/time shims); whether the Android build behaves differently at this step. **Next step: v23 (see Turn 29 next steps in section 11).**

- **STATUS AT TURN 28 (2026-11-01) [CONFIRMED from three loader logs, two iOS reports, `p2.log`]: the Turn 27 silent death is SOLVED; the game now runs further and dies again, later, at a different place.** Cause of the Turn 27 death: a game thread called `sscanf`, which the loader had never shimmed (Darwin reads variadic arguments from the stack, Android passes them in registers, so Darwin read garbage pointers and faulted), AND the game's own `sigaction(SIGSEGV, ...)` went straight to Darwin with **Bionic's struct layout**, so Darwin took `sa_flags` (= `SA_SIGINFO` = 4) as the handler ADDRESS and replaced the loader's crash handler; the SIGSEGV from `sscanf` then jumped to pc=0x4 and iOS killed the process (`EXC_BAD_ACCESS`, `SIGKILL`, termination namespace `CODESIGNING` "Invalid Page"), leaving no line in our log. **v21** (scanf-family shims + sigaction guard) fixes both: the game gets past `state 5 target 2`, runs its new-game/ability setup (`abilities locked:` lines) to `state 6 target 2 stack [ 2 ]`, then **our handler logs `CRASH ... sig=10 fault=0x7800000000 pc=elf+0x5367168 lr=elf+0x536710c`** (fault = the guest base = guest address 0 [INFERRED null dereference]; **cause UNKNOWN**). **v22** (`build-loader-v22.yml`) adds a full register / instruction-word / stack / frame-chain dump to that CRASH line; written, **not yet built or run**. Device and OS (CONFIRMED by the `.ips`): iPhone18,2, iPhone OS 27.0 (24A437), developer mode on.

  (3) **`P2Loader-2026-11-01-100233.ips` (bug_type 309) [CONFIRMED fields]:** `procLaunch 10:02:17`, crash 10:02:32 (about 15 s). `exception`: `EXC_BAD_ACCESS`, `signal SIGKILL`, `KERN_PROTECTION_FAILURE at 0x4`; `termination`: namespace `CODESIGNING`, code 2, indicator "Invalid Page"; `ktriageinfo`: "Failed to fault in a page with execute permissions". Faulting thread 12 (a game thread started through our `thr_tramp`). Frame 0 = **pc 0x4**, then `_sigtramp`, `__svfscanf_l`, `vsscanf_l`, `sscanf`, then ~19 guest frames (P2Code, elf offsets from 0x5ac5af0 outward), then `thr_tramp`, `_pthread_start`. Registers: `x0 = 0xb` (SIGSEGV), `x1`/`x2` = siginfo/ucontext, `sp` on the alternate signal stack. **Reading:** a SIGSEGV hit the thread inside Darwin's `sscanf`; the kernel called the handler registered for SIGSEGV, whose address was 4. The Bionic `struct sigaction` is `{int sa_flags @0; handler @8; mask @16; restorer @24}`, Darwin's is `{handler @0; mask @8; flags @12}`: Darwin read the game's `sa_flags` (= 4 = Linux `SA_SIGINFO`) as the handler and the high half of the real handler pointer (= 1 = Darwin `SA_ONSTACK`) as flags, which also explains why the handler ran on the alternate stack. **The handler-address half is CONFIRMED by the registers; the `sscanf` half is [INFERRED, strong]**: `sscanf` is not in the shim table, the loader shims the whole printf family for exactly this ABI reason, and the fault is inside `__svfscanf_l`; the fault address inside `sscanf` was lost with the overwritten handler, so it is not directly proven.
  (4) **v21 written (`build-loader-v21.yml`, from the user's v20)** [YAML parses; pure-logic pieces unit-tested on x86 (item 8); iOS compile and run later CONFIRMED by the v21 log]: (a) `bio_scan_slots` + `sh_sscanf/fscanf/scanf/vsscanf/vfscanf/vscanf` (same technique as the printf shims: named register-file params, rebuild a Darwin `va_list`; every scanf argument is a pointer so only general-purpose slots are needed; `%*`, `%[..]`, `%n` handled); (b) `sh_sigaction` / `sh_signal`: Bionic struct layout read correctly, Linux-to-Darwin signal numbers mapped (SIGBUS 7 to 10, SIGUSR1 10 to 30, SIGCHLD 17 to 20, SIGSYS 31 to 12, ...), the request **recorded and logged but NOT installed for fault signals (ILL, TRAP, ABRT, BUS, FPE, SEGV)** so the loader's own crash handler stays; SIG_DFL/SIG_IGN for other signals are applied; (c) `resolve()` logs `IMPORT RISK: <name>` for imports that fall straight through to Darwin although the ABI differs; (d) header `== P2 loader v21 ==`.

  (9) **v22 written (`build-loader-v22.yml`, on top of v21)** [YAML parses; portable helpers compiled and run on x86 with stubs; **Apple-only branch NOT compiled; not built or run on the device**]: `dump_ctx()` is called from `crash()` after the existing CRASH line and writes (to both logs, fsync'd) **x0-x28, fp/lr/sp/pc; "fault 0x... is guest address 0x..." when the fault lies in the guest window; 20 instruction words from pc-64 (word #16 is pc) read from the loaded code so they can be disassembled offline; 12 stack words at sp; a frame-pointer chain as elf offsets**. Every memory read goes through `vm_read_overwrite` so a bad pointer cannot fault inside the handler. The code-size bound `0x5b2c000` is hard-coded (equals the data-code delta of this SHA-locked build). Header `== P2 loader v22 ==`. **What to look for:** which register is 0 (or near 0) at the faulting instruction, the instruction itself (decode by hand or via CI), and the caller chain.

The 16 callbacks are exactly the `android_native_app_glue` set; in that glue `onStart/onResume/onInputQueueCreated/onNativeWindowCreated` write a command to a pipe and **block the activity thread until the game thread has read it**, and the game thread learns of it via `ALooper_addFd(msgread, ident=1)` + `ALooper_pollOnce`. v15's `ALooper_addFd` ignored its arguments and `pollOnce` always returned TIMEOUT, so the handshake could never finish: a deadlock on the loader's main thread, which also froze the UI (the user's \"shows nothing\"). **v16 changes (only these):** (a) **real per-thread looper**: `ALooper_prepare` returns a per-thread object, `ALooper_addFd` stores fd/ident/events/callback/data (16 slots, logs the first 12), `ALooper_pollOnce` uses Darwin `poll()` in <=16 ms slices, returns the source's ident with fd/events/data (Darwin->Android event bits mapped), runs callback sources (returns -2, removes the source if the callback returns 0), TIMEOUT -3 otherwise; (b) **the loader runs on its own 16 MB-stack NSThread**; `L()` hands UI text to the main queue (`dispatch_async_f`), the app coalesces updates and auto-scrolls; Run can be pressed once per launch (message tells the user to relaunch to repeat); (c) **crash handler works for any thread**: writes `CRASH (thread, stage) sig fault pc(elf+off) lr(elf+off)` to BOTH log files with fsync; (d) **stage tracking + watchdog**: each lifecycle call sets a stage name; if the stage does not change for 3/10/30 s the log gets `WATCHDOG: stage \"X\" has not changed ... (looper polls N, polls that returned a source M)`; (e) stage-2 wait raised from 20 s to 30 s, summary line prints looper counters. **Tested [CONFIRMED in sandbox]:** YAML parses; `bash -n` on every run step; `p2core.c` `gcc -fsyntax-only` with a fake `layout.h` (only the old style warnings; Apple-only code paths not compiled); the looper block extracted and run on x86 (empty poll, addFd, ident+data returned, callback source -2, callback removal, hangup bit). **NOT tested:** iOS compile of `p2core.c` and `main.m` (no SDK), anything on device. **What to look for in the next device log:** `PLAT ALooper_addFd ...` lines, `PLAT ALooper_pollOnce returns ident=...`, then `onStart: returned`, `onResume: returned`, ... (each lifecycle line), any `ANDROID LOG` lines from the game thread, any `CRASH (thread ..., stage ...)`, any `WATCHDOG` line. **Likely next walls [INFERRED]:** `onNativeWindowCreated` makes the game thread start the renderer (`dlopen(\"libvulkan.so\")` still fails; `ANativeWindow_lock` still fails by design), so expect a crash/exit there; that is the cue to build the MoltenVK step (verify the current release asset by web search first) and the CPU `ANativeWindow_lock` buffer for `DrawMessageScreen`. **Limitation:** one `ALooper` per thread is modelled; `ALooper_wake`/`removeFd` are not imported by the library so not provided.

- **Update (Turn 23) [CONFIRMED: v13 run on device, `p2loader_v13.txt`; v14 WRITTEN, syntax-checked only]:** (1) **v13 results:** unresolved imports 70 -> 52 (all remaining are NDK `A*` platform functions plus `__android_log_print` and `__fwrite_chk`, which stay logging stubs). `.init_array`: [0]-[14] return, [20] and [26] return, **[15],[16],[17],[18],[19],[21],[22],[23],[24] die with the abort message "... failed to acquire mutex"** (prefix of the message is garbage bytes, e.g. "–5q failed to acquire mutex" [INFERRED: the host formats the message with a variadic call that Darwin reads wrongly, see variadic note below]), and **[25] "terminating due to  exception of type ‡Ópm: "** (the C++ type name is garbled; same suspicion). Crash kinds unchanged: sig 11 fault 0x0 inside a system library (x4) and sig 6 abort (x5+). **[INFERRED] root cause of "failed to acquire mutex":** Bionic's `pthread_mutex_t` is 40 bytes, statically initialised to ZEROS; Darwin's needs a signature word and is 64 bytes, so `pthread_mutex_lock` on a zero-initialised Bionic mutex fails with EINVAL (or corrupts neighbours). Same problem for cond (48 vs 48+sig), rwlock, `pthread_once_t` (int vs 16 bytes), `pthread_key_t` (int vs 8 bytes), `pthread_attr_t` (56 vs 64), `pthread_mutexattr_t` (int vs 64-byte struct, init would overwrite guest memory), errno numbers (ETIMEDOUT 110 vs 60, EAGAIN 11 vs 35, EDEADLK 35 vs 11), clock ids (Linux MONOTONIC=1 vs Darwin 6; id 1 is invalid on Darwin), `sysconf` constants, and `mmap`/`madvise` flag values (MAP_ANONYMOUS 0x20 vs 0x1000; MADV_FREE 8 vs 5). (2) **v14 (`build-loader-v14.yml`)** = v13 + `shim_real` entries for all of those: lazily created Darwin objects hung off the guest object (pointer at +8, CAS, so zero-initialised static mutexes work; recursive/errorcheck type read from the Bionic state bits 14-15), Darwin->Linux errno translation on pthread returns, guest attr structs handled without calling Darwin attr functions on them, `pthread_create` with a >=1 MB stack, `pthread_setname_np`, translating `clock_gettime`, `sysconf` (page size 16384, CPU count), `mmap` (flag translation) and `madvise`. **Only names that are actually imported take effect.** Not verified on device. (3) **Logging (user request):** the app now writes **`p2_loader_log.txt` at the root of the app's Documents folder (latest run, overwritten) and `p2_loader_log_YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.txt` (one per run)**, every line flushed with fsync so a hard crash keeps the log. Find them in the Files app under On My iPhone > P2Loader (file sharing and in-place opening are enabled in Info.plist). The "Copy report" button still works. (4) **Still open:** variadic ABI (Darwin passes variadic args on the stack, Android in registers: `snprintf`/`printf`/`open(mode)`/`fcntl`/`ioctl` called by the guest read garbage) needs asm trampolines; `__android_log_print`/`__fwrite_chk` still logging stubs; the 52 NDK platform functions (the host's platform layer, see §4c) are the real port work.

  (3) **What the dev says about the project (user's screenshots, dev "Elite"; supersedes my inference in §4):** "static recomp, not a source port"; written in C++; "I translate the game into native ARM from pseudo code" (decompiler-style pseudocode, **not** necessarily x86 machine code as §4 assumed); the **"host" = controls, audio, optimizations**, closed source, "Prototype 2 is the latest version of the host" (an earlier host build was used for NFS: Most Wanted 2012); **the renderer is rewritten from scratch "based on its own engine"** (so the D3D9/Win32-shim description in §4 may be wrong; re-check against the real imports); "no one tap port option", finding addresses and wiring them to the host takes time per game.   (3b) **Licensing note raised with the user:** the host is the dev's closed-source work and the game code/assets are third-party; running privately is one thing, publishing a patched build is another. Repo is private and `build-loader` refuses public repos.
  (4) **JIT question (user: can enable JIT with StikDebug + LocalDevVPN; asked if jitless hurts performance/battery).** Answer given: **no cost.** The recompiled code is already native ARM64, so loading it statically vs. mapping it executable at runtime gives the *same instructions at the same speed*; JIT only pays off for emulators/translators that generate code. Static loads need no debugger attached (less overhead, normal launch, shareable without JIT setup). The 613 stubs + 10 trampolines add a few jumps; the 1.8 M base patches are in-place constants (no extra instructions). **JIT would not remove the real blockers:** the 0x2000000000 window (address space), TPIDR_EL0 ownership, the 70 Android imports, TLSDESC. What JIT would simplify: loading the original `.so` without embedding it. **[UNVERIFIED]** no game code has run yet, so there is no measured performance number; real FPS/battery will depend on the host/renderer.
  (5) **Next:** TLSDESC resolver (31 sites + 37 relocs), compat shims for the 70 imports, `.init_array`, first boot. v11 is the build to keep.

Block at **0x5b2b580**, 2,624 bytes left in the code-page tail. **Patched SHA (v10): `3f2f4f0120320e8ecc36b5fa4600d01c0ae94b1c5751c6a82a1552e193482979`.** On device: code=0x10f6b8000, data-code OK, relocs identical, canary block `OK`, both demangle calls pass, `TPIDR_EL0=0x1004` again.
  (2) **CORRECTION of Turn 18 items (3) and (5):** I said the `cbz x8` sites in `GetLastErrorValue/SetLastError/HostFatal` (and the `tbz` in `NullVbUnlocked`) test the thread pointer as a value. **Wrong.** The context shows `mrs x8,tpidr_el0; ldr x8,[x8,x0]; cbz x8,...`: the load **overwrites x8**, so the `cbz` tests the **loaded TLS value** (a pointer to per-thread game data), not the thread pointer. They are ordinary TLSDESC accesses and were false refusals of v10 (its TLS rule did not treat a load into xN as a kill).

  (2) **Build (v8): the conservative scan patched only 5 of 613 `mrs` sites, refused 608.** Reasons: **399 "no sized function around site"** (those sites are in functions with no sized dynsym entry), `ldp` 131, `ldr` 35, `str` 27, `ldrb` 8, `mov` 3, `add` 2, `stp` 2, `strb` 1. Patched SHA of that (incomplete) build: `20c4274253f04199a485dd17a1357edb337c7af720cf7a54578f541b5cbd09e7`; orr/movk/movz counts and layout unchanged; `ands` line now correctly 0. **The real `llvm-objdump` output parsed fine** (the report shows real instruction text), which validates the parser.
  (3) **Diagnosis:** (a) function bounds from dynsym are not enough; unexported functions exist, so use **`.eh_frame` FDEs** (C++ exceptions are used, every function should have one). (b) The rule was over-strict: `ldp/stp/str xN,[sp,..]` are callee-saved **spills/restores** (a load into xN also *kills* it), and **`[xN, xM]` register-offset access (e.g. `ldr w1,[x22,x0]`) is exactly the TLSDESC pattern: `mrs tp; ldr [tp, resolver_offset]`**. That works with a constant block address if our TLSDESC resolver returns `(per-thread slot address) - block address`. So those sites are fine to patch and tell us how many sites need the resolver.
  (4) **v9 (`build-loader-v9.yml`) = v8 + FDE bounds + relaxed rule.** Safe uses: `ldr xT,[xN,#0x28]` (canary); `[xN,xM{,ext}]` and `add xY,xN,xM` (TLS); `str/stp xN` spills; a load into xN or `add xN,xN,xM` ends the scan. Still refused: any other offset (`[xN]`, `[xN,#imm]`), `add/sub #imm`, `mov` copies (3 seen; cannot track), compares, w-form stores, stores through the block (read-only, would fault loudly). **Residual risk [UNVERIFIED]:** a spilled xN reloaded into another register and then used at a non-0x28 immediate offset is not detectable; the block is zero-filled so such a read gives 0, not garbage. v9 prints per-kind counts, the number of TLS sites, and up to 40 refused sites with the offending instruction.
  (5) **Design consequence for the TLS step:** with TP = (loaded code base + block offset), a TLSDESC resolver must return `thread_block + var_offset - TP`, any 64-bit value; no per-thread TPIDR is needed.

  (3) **TPIDR probe: `TPIDR_EL0=0x1004`, `TPIDRRO_EL0=0x1f07ea960`.** TPIDR_EL0 is **non-zero on this device, so the probe correctly refused to write it** (the survive-20-sleeps / second-thread checks therefore did not run). Something already uses TPIDR_EL0 (what, **[UNVERIFIED]**: not identified). **Consequence: Option A (own TPIDR_EL0 block per thread) is dropped**, writing it could corrupt whatever owns it. TPIDRRO_EL0 holds a pointer that looks like Darwin's per-thread TSD base (**[INFERRED]**).
  (4) **Option B (re-point `mrs` to `tpidrro_el0`) is risky [INFERRED, verify before use]:** the canary would be read from TSD slot 5 (+0x28), a slot Darwin may write (e.g. kernel/workqueue flags); if it changes while a guarded function is live, `__stack_chk_fail` fires falsely.
  (5) **Decision (Turn 16): Option C-lite, "shared constant block".** Each of the 613 `mrs xN,tpidr_el0` becomes `b cave`; cave = `adrp xN,blk; add xN,xN,#:lo12:blk; b site+4` (3 instructions + nop = 16 bytes). `blk` is a small **read-only** block in the code-page tail with a constant 64-bit canary at +0x28. A single global canary is fine for stack-protector semantics (only prologue/epilogue consistency matters). **Space:** 613 x 16 = 9,808 bytes + block (~0x30-0x40) vs free tail 12,544 - 160 = 12,384 bytes, so it fits with ~2.4 KB spare, data-page layout unchanged (assert it). **Branch range:** farthest site (.text start 0x9c77a0) to the caves (0x5b28fxx) is about 85 MB < 128 MB, OK. **Precondition [UNVERIFIED for 613 sites, only 2 sampled]:** every site only uses xN for `ldr [xN,#0x28]`. The v8 patcher must scan the instructions after each site (until xN is overwritten) and **refuse to patch (list the site) if xN is stored through, offset by anything other than 0x28, or passed on**. Real TLS goes through the 37 TLSDESC relocs (our resolver), **[INFERRED]** not through inline `mrs`. Fallback for any refused site: per-site decision after seeing the list.
  (6) **CORRECTION (Turn 16, later): the "1 `ands` site" was a phantom, and my first Turn 16 diagnosis (`tst` blind spot) was wrong.** The v6/v7 summary line counted logical-immediate words with mask `0x9F400000`, which also matches **MOVK/MOVZ with hw>=2** (move-wide is `100101` in bits 28:23, the mask ignored bit 23). A `movk xd,#0xD8xx,lsl#32` has immr=27/imms=0/opc=3 and was counted as `ands`. The extra scan used the correct mask and found **0** sites. Proven by encoding `0xF2DB0000` (movk x0,#0xD800,lsl#32) under both masks. v8 uses mask `0x9FC00000`; the orr count is unaffected (opc 1 has no move-wide encoding), so all earlier orr/movk patch counts stand. **So there is no unpatched `ands`/`tst` site; the Turn 15 item (5) is closed.**

See the v8 entry in §11. **Sandbox note:** this session's sandbox had **no clang/lld/llvm-objdump/aarch64 binutils** (only x86 binutils + Python/numpy), contrary to the Turn 13 note, so v8 was tested with a hand-built synthetic aarch64 ELF plus a fake objdump, not a real toolchain.

(2) **`#0x21` window-end constants: 0 sites.** (3) `and x0,x1,#0x7fffffffff` x1 at 0x5a9ef84 masks a 39-bit pointer; the new base + 4 GB (< 0x7900000000) stays under 2^39, so it is safe as is. (4) **REAL FINDING: the 10 `orr xd,xzr,#0x2000000020` sites are base|0x20 constants that v5/v6 did NOT patch** (the patcher only rewrites the exact immediate 0x2000000000). They are used as `mov x8,#B|0x20; ...; movk x8,#0xf04,lsl#16; ldar x8,[x8]` (host address of guest 0x0f040020) and as an index register (`ldr q0,[x26,x9]`: guest pointer + base + 0x20). Left alone they would address 0x2000...., which is not mapped on the phone. They **cannot be re-encoded in place**: a brute force over all 4 GB-aligned bases shows **0x2000000000 is the only base where both base and base|0x20 are single AArch64 logical immediates** (coincidence: bit 37 is bit 5 modulo 32). **Fix in `build-loader-v7.yml`:** each site becomes `b cave`; cave = `movz xd,#0x20; movk xd,#0x78,lsl#32; b site+4` (3 instructions + nop, 16 bytes each), caves are appended to `code.bin` in the free tail of the code page (0x5b28f00 to 0x5b2c000 = 12,544 free bytes, so the data-page layout does not change; asserted), all sites verified to be plain `mov` (Rn = xzr). Tested on a synthetic aarch64 ELF (disassembled output checked: branch targets and return addresses correct); not yet run on the real library. **Patched SHA will change** (record the new one from the v7 report). (5) ~~`ands` x1 unpatched~~ **RETRACTED in Turn 16: phantom, a MOVK counted by a loose mask; 0 such sites.** (6) **Thread pointer [CONFIRMED]: 613 `mrs xN,tpidr_el0` sites, 0 `mrs tpidrro_el0`, 0 `msr tpidr_el0`; sampled contexts (0xd64720, 0x5a9ef88) show `ldr x8,[xN,#0x28]` right after, i.e. the Android stack-protector canary is read from TPIDR_EL0+0x28.** `__cxa_demangle` apparently has no canary, which is why Turn 14's test did not crash. Any guarded function will read `[TPIDR_EL0+0x28]`; if iOS leaves TPIDR_EL0 at 0 that faults at address 0x28. Options (decide after the on-device probe): **A** keep the code unmodified and set TPIDR_EL0 per thread to our own block (canary at +0x28, TLS), if iOS preserves it across context switches; **B** patch the 613 sites in place to `mrs xN,tpidrro_el0` (same size; canary then comes from `pthread_t+0x28`, stability **unverified**; TLS-descriptor resolvers must return `block - tpidrro`); **C** per-site trampolines. **v7 adds a safe on-device probe** (reads both registers; writes TPIDR_EL0 only if it is 0; checks the value survives 20 sleeps and a second thread writing its own value; restores 0). (7) The 124 `(0,0x20)` 32-bit word pairs in the file, 106 only 4-byte aligned, leave exactly the 18 aligned literals, confirming why v4 said 138. (8) Relocation/layout lines identical to v5.

Written: `build-loader-v5.yml` (private-repo guard; patches a copy; builds `P2Code.framework` = code blob in `__TEXT` + zerofill `__DATA` at +0x5b2c000, verified by `nm`; test app applies RELA/RELR relocs, resolves imports by `dlsym`, calls `__cxa_demangle`; crash handler prints pc as elf+offset; report in Documents/p2_loader_report.txt). **Not compiled (no iOS SDK in sandbox); `prep.py` tested on synthetic inputs only.** Open risks: linker places `__DATA` elsewhere (workflow retries with `-segaddr`), sideload tool may not sign the nested framework, Bionic-vs-Darwin ABI for resolved imports.
- **Update (Turn 11) [CONFIRMED probe v3, iPhone18,2 / iOS 27.0, run with StikDebug attached AND launched normally]:** identical results both ways, so the debugger is **not** why `0x2000000000` fails (`vm_allocate FIXED` → NO_SPACE, something system-mapped; my region list was capped at 60 rows so the occupant is not identified). **`0x7000000000`-`0x10000000000` (≥576 GB) is free; 4 GB RW windows at `0x7000000000` and `0x7800000000` both succeed** with first/last 64 MB touched. Memory ramp 6 GB OK again. `mmap(NULL)` hands out `0x7000000000` first, so **chosen new guest base = `0x7800000000`** (32 GB above the default allocation point; bits 35-38 = valid logical immediate, ORR N=1 imms=3 immr=29; movk/movz imm16 = 0x78). The window must still be reserved first thing at launch. Written: `patch-apk-v4.yml` (dry run: patches a copy in CI, verifies counts, uploads only a report; classification of the 49,432 `movk` sites is the open risk). Next: build a dylib (`__TEXT` blob + zerofill `__DATA` at the exact ELF offsets, `-segaddr`), loader smoke test calling a harmless export (e.g. libc++ `__cxa_demangle`). **Repo must be private before any build that embeds `libp2re` code is uploaded.**
- **Update (Turn 10) [CONFIRMED scan-v3 on libp2re.so 1.0.3]:** **No relocation of any kind targets the executable segment** (751 `.rela.dyn` + 256 `.rela.plt` all hit data; `DF_TEXTREL` false; RELR not decoded in the scan output, see note). So the 84.6 MB code can be embedded **unmodified** (apart from the guest-base patch) in a signed Mach-O; only data needs relocation at runtime. **Guest-base sites: 1,811,267 `orr xd,xn,#0x2000000000`, 49,432 `movk xd,#0x20,lsl#32`, 114 `movz xd,#0x20,lsl#32`**, all fixed-size and patchable offline (verified encodings: `0x7000000000` = ORR N=1 imms=2 immr=28; `0x7800000000` = imms=3 immr=29). Segment gap between code and data is exactly 0x4000. **Correction of my Turn 9 claim:** I said JIT was "unreliable" on the user's iPhone. That was wrong. On TXM devices (iOS 26+) the *app* must cooperate with StikDebug via `brk #0xf00d` (x16 command 1 = prepare region, 0 = detach, 2 = scripts; legacy `brk #0x69`), and my probe did not speak that protocol; P_TRACED=YES showed the attach worked. User has used JIT for years and supplied `universal.js`, `legacy.js`, `maciOS.js`, `attachDetach.js`. **Decision (Turn 10): JIT-free static embedding is the primary route; the brk-protocol JIT loader is the fallback.** Reasons: game code does no runtime codegen so JIT gives no speed benefit; no StikDebug/LocalDevVPN needed per launch; file-backed signed pages are evictable. **Open:** what occupies 0x1000000000-0x6000000000 and whether it differs with the debugger detached (probe v3 maps it). Repo visibility still unknown; any IPA containing `libp2re` code must stay in a **private** repo.
- **Update (Turn 9) [CONFIRMED by probe v2 on iPhone18,2 / iOS 27.0, 11.4 GB RAM]:** (1) Memory is fine: 6 GB touched, no kill. (2) **Fixed window at `0x2000000000` is occupied** (`vm_allocate FIXED` → KERN_NO_SPACE for 0x1000000000, 0x2000000000, 0x4000000000, 0x6000000000), but **0x7000000000 and 0x7800000000 accept a full 4 GB**. Entitled IPA not yet tested. (3) JIT: debugger attached (P_TRACED yes), `MAP_JIT` EPERM, `mprotect` RX allowed but executing the stub hung (watchdog fired). JIT on this iOS 27/TXM-class device is unreliable. (4) **User pointed out JIT shouldn't be needed**, and is right: the game code is AOT native ARM64; JIT was only for *our* runtime ELF loader. **New primary plan: JIT-free** (§6a-bis). (5) Discord dev ("Elite") says: static recomp, **host is closed source**, C++ output, renderer rewritten per game, wiring addresses per game. So Option B (get host source) is effectively dead; Option C (re-lift) needs his tool. User reports other jitless ports (GTA V, nxnative projects) run on their phone.
- **Update (Turn 8):** Wrote `build-probe.yml`, a **single self-contained workflow** (all source embedded) that builds the probe app on `macos-latest` and uploads two unsigned IPAs (`P2Probe-plain.ipa`, `P2Probe-entitled.ipa`). **Not yet run.** Next: user creates the file in their repo, runs it, sideloads, runs the probe, pastes the report back. See §11 "Turn 8".
- **Update (Turn 7):** The v2 analysis ran successfully. It confirmed a pure ahead-of-time design (no JIT in the game itself), a tiny import surface (257 symbols), tiny relocation tables, and that the runtime is a **multi-game recompilation framework** (strings mention *Far Cry 3* and *NFSRE* too). MoltenVK now supports Vulkan 1.3 (verified by search, §4b). See §4b and §6a.
- **Update (Turn 5):** The user found the **Discord server the APK comes from** and a newer build (**v1.0.3**, versionCode 4) with bug fixes. No public git repo has been spotted yet. The user **can enable JIT** on their iPhone (sideload JIT tools, including an in-app option via LocalDevVPN), which makes a **runtime ELF loader (Option A2)** realistic again. See §6.
- **Most valuable unknown:** Is the runtime (and ideally the recompiler) source available *anywhere*, not necessarily a public git repo (a zip in Discord, or the authors willing to share)? If so the path becomes much easier. See §8.

## 1. User goal and constraints

| Item | Detail |
|---|---|
| Game | *Prototype 2* (Radical Entertainment / Activision, 2012, originally PC/Xbox 360/PS3) |
| Motivation | Better performance and battery life than running the PC version through an emulator on the iPhone |
| Prior setup | Currently emulating via "Madeira" (user's word; emulator on iPhone) |
| Wants | Game to "speak native Metal" and run natively. Does **not** want an approach that watches what the Android port does each frame and recreates the visuals |
| Open to | Wrapping the renderer in Metal (e.g. Vulkan→Metal layer), or "porting the port" if that is what the evidence supports (user said this explicitly) |
| Rejected | Streaming (Sunshine/Moonlight/Steam Link). Do not suggest again |
| Platform | iPhone only. Mac is at a pawn shop. Device model and iOS version **unknown** (ask) |
| Skills | Not technically inept, not a software developer. Self-described "vibe coder". Wants step-by-step instructions doable on a phone |
| Game files | Not included in the APK. The user must supply their own PC game files (see FolderPicker in §5) |
| Time/context | Working late at night, on mobile. Prefers short, actionable steps |

**Hard user preference:** Keep answers practical, honest, and phone-friendly. Don't push alternatives the user already rejected.

## 2. Environment and tooling

- **No Mac.** Builds happen on GitHub Actions. `macos-latest` runners can run Xcode, `xcodebuild`, `clang`, etc.
- **GitHub repo:** `https://github.com/Mattieus-sudo/poisonous-mausoleum` (user's repo). Its README links to a decrypted APK hosted externally (Google Drive).
- **APK source used for inspection:** Google Drive share link `https://drive.google.com/file/d/1QcHUrrsNjlb2BeH_MHkgUCuFYDbw_kzZ/view?usp=share_link` (downloaded in CI via `gdown --fuzzy`).
- **Agent sandbox limitation (Claude):** no network from the code sandbox, cannot fetch the GitHub repo or Drive file directly. All APK analysis must be done by **GitHub Actions workflows**, with results pasted or uploaded back by the user.
- **Installing on device (partly unsolved):** unsigned IPA needs signing. Options to evaluate: SideStore/AltStore (may need one-time computer setup), TrollStore (only some iOS versions), or a paid Apple Developer account (cloud signing in CI).
- **JIT [user-reported, Turn 5]:** The user says they have JIT-enabling capability on their iPhone, with "leagues of apps" available and an in-app JIT enabling flow when connected to **LocalDevVPN**. They don't yet know exactly how to wire it into our app. **[UNVERIFIED by us]**: exactly which tool/method, the iOS version, and whether the app must carry specific entitlements (e.g. `get-task-allow`) or call `MAP_JIT`/debugger-attach-aware code. Verify the current method with web search when we implement A2, and ask for iOS version + device model + which JIT app they use.

1. **Turn 1.** User pasted screenshots of a plan from a local model (Qwen 3.5 9B "Uncensored"). The plan was **largely wrong**: it assumed Java decompilation + Hopper on iOS + rewriting logic in Metal. Corrections in §9.
2. **Turn 2.** Claude gave an honest assessment and proposed two tracks (streaming vs native). User **rejected streaming** and chose the native track.
3. **Turn 3.** Claude wrote `inspect-apk.yml`, a GitHub Actions `workflow_dispatch` workflow that downloads the APK, unzips it, runs `aapt dump badging`, `readelf`, `nm`, `strings`, and jadx, then uploads `report.md` + `jadx_out` as an artifact.
4. **Turn 4.** The workflow ran successfully (26 s). User uploaded `apk-inspection.zip` and two log screenshots. Findings in §4–5. User asked for this handoff file to be maintained from now on.
5. **Turn 5.** User reported: JIT is available on their phone; they found the Discord the APK came from, a newer build exists, and no git link is visible there. They re-ran the v1 workflow on the new APK and uploaded `apk-inspection_2.zip`. **Diff vs. v1: only a minor rebuild** (see §4a). Claude wrote `inspect-apk-v2.yml` (deep static analysis, syntax-checked locally but **not yet run**) and clarified what "source code" means (see §8).
6. **Turn 6.** v2 first run failed (HTML saved as APK by a pasted download rewrite). Fixed (see "Download pitfalls").
7. **Turn 7 (this one).** v2 ran successfully; analysis in §4b/§6a.

### Workflow quirks observed (all harmless so far)
- `sort: write failed: Broken pipe` in "Build report": from `| sort | uniq -c | sort -rn | head`. Harmless.
- jadx "progress: 0 of 1 (0%)" and only one Java class produced. Expected, the dex is only 3,036 bytes.
- Artifact upload worked. The user retrieves it from the run page and sends it back as a zip.
- **Known false positives in the strings grep:** my regex included `d3d9`, which matched function names like `_Z10f_105d3d90P3Cpu` (the substring `5d3d90`). Those lines are *not* D3D9 evidence. They are still useful evidence of the naming scheme (§5).

---

### Download pitfalls (learned Turn 6)
- **v2's first run FAILED at "Unzip APK"** (`End-of-central-directory signature not found`): the download step "succeeded" in 1 s but saved a **web page, not an APK**.
- **Cause (most likely):** the user replaced the download step with a Google-AI rewrite whose `case` pattern `*://google.com*` **does not match** `https://drive.google.com/...`, so it fell through to plain `curl`, which saved Drive's HTML viewer page. The old `unzip -tq ... | tail -1` hid the failure because a pipe returns the last command's exit code.
- **`--fuzzy` was not the problem.** It worked in both v1 runs. (It is a `gdown` option, not a git one.) The user still prefers it removed, so the v2 workflow now extracts the file ID from the share link with `sed` and calls `gdown "https://drive.google.com/uc?id=<ID>"`.
- v2 now **fails loudly** if the downloaded file isn't a valid zip and prints the first 400 bytes.
- **Convention:** put the user's current APK link as the workflow's `apk_url` default so they don't paste it each run. **Current link (user says "new", believed to be 1.0.3):** `https://drive.google.com/file/d/1cSoNW0PKW_WHhULqHcKUMzv3bOpOuPan/view?usp=share_link`. The older 1.0.2 link was `.../1QcHUrrsNjlb2BeH_MHkgUCuFYDbw_kzZ/...`. Put the current link in any future workflow.
- The separate "Unzip APK" step also existed in v1; it isn't new. Later steps read the extracted `apk/` folder.

## 4. Confirmed facts (from `report.md`)

### APK
- Package: `com.p2re.prototype2`, label "Prototype 2", versionName `1.0.2`, versionCode `3`
- minSdk 29, targetSdk 35, compileSdk 35
- Native ABI: **arm64-v8a only**
- Launchable activity: **`android.app.NativeActivity`** (no custom launcher Java; the whole app is native)
- Permissions: `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (all-files access); legacy `READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` (≤ SDK 32)
- APK size: 44,486,638 bytes (44 MB compressed)
- Contents: `AndroidManifest.xml`, `classes.dex` (3,036 bytes), `lib/arm64-v8a/libp2re.so` (97,013,032 bytes), `assets/` (including `msvcr90.dll` 656 KB and an `assets/spirv/` directory of precompiled shaders), `res/`, `META-INF/` (signature files named `P2.*`)

### Native library `libp2re.so`
- ELF 64-bit ARM aarch64, dynamically linked, **stripped**, BuildID `39eb5891bdf27c9e59710180d073e384b88f0b78`
- **NEEDED libs (only seven):** `libandroid.so`, `liblog.so`, `libaaudio.so`, `libdl.so`, `libz.so`, `libm.so`, `libc.so`
  - Notably absent: `libvulkan.so` (loaded at runtime via `dlopen`; strings show "ngfx: loading Vulkan", "libvulkan.so"), `libEGL`, `libGLES*`, `libOpenSLES`, `libc++_shared` (so libc++ is statically linked)
- **82,141 exported dynamic symbols**
- **No `Java_*` JNI entry points.** No JNI bridge, so native code does not call into Java except via NativeActivity glue (to be verified)
- Built with clang/LLVM (`out/llvm-project/libcxxabi/...` demangler paths in strings)

### 4a. Build 1.0.3 vs 1.0.2 (Turn 5 diff, **[CONFIRMED]**)
| | 1.0.2 (vc 3) | 1.0.3 (vc 4) |
|---|---|---|
| APK size | 44,486,638 | 44,490,734 |
| `libp2re.so` size | 97,013,032 | 97,017,856 (+4,824 bytes) |
| BuildID | `39eb5891bdf27c9e59710180d073e384b88f0b78` | `786bd9c3e37d4abcaad1058a81ca1a75dfb6c0e8` |
| Exported symbols | 82,141 | 82,143 (+2) |
| `classes.dex` | 3,036 bytes | 3,036 bytes (same size) |
| Everything else in report (NEEDED libs, permissions, SDK levels, strings listed, assets top-60) | | unchanged |

Conclusion: 1.0.3 is a small bug-fix rebuild. **Use 1.0.3 as the working baseline** from now on; all earlier findings still apply.

### 4b. v2 deep analysis of `libp2re.so`, build 1.0.3 (Turn 7, **[CONFIRMED]** from `apk-inspection-v2.zip`)

**Identity.** `game.apk` SHA-256 `66f7d03437f5f8bf8f4dd1e97237007d304f4880cf271a17b6a411d78efdd896`; `libp2re.so` SHA-256 `d904d2cba7869bb9feb800e0a80e77948cc9236203fcee9d332952f91ba90640`; BuildID `786bd9c3e37d4abcaad1058a81ca1a75dfb6c0e8`.

**Manifest.** NativeActivity with `android.app.lib_name = p2re`; `screenOrientation=6` (sensor landscape); `largeHeap`, `isGame`, `extractNativeLibs=true`, `profileable shell`; requires `android.hardware.vulkan.version` 0x401000 (Vulkan **1.1** as a store filter; the code itself demands a **1.3** device at runtime); gamepad optional; `FolderPicker` activity (not exported).

**ELF layout** (page alignment 0x4000 = 16 KB, same as iOS):
| Segment | VMA | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| LOAD R+E | 0x0 – 0x5b28f00 | dynsym/dynstr/gnu.hash, `.rela.dyn`, `.relr.dyn`, `.rela.plt`, `.rodata` (~1.2 MB), `.gcc_except_table`, `.eh_frame_hdr`, `.eh_frame` (~3.9 MB), **`.text` at 0x9c77a0, size 0x516062c (~84.6 MB)**, `__lcxx_override`, `.plt` |
| LOAD RW | 0x5b2cf00 (filesz 0x144178) | `.tdata`, `.data.rel.ro` (~1.3 MB, function-pointer tables), fini/init arrays, `.dynamic`, `.got`, `.got.plt` |
| LOAD RW | 0x5c75078 (memsz 0x3c4b30) | `.data` (0x186d0), `.bss` (~3.8 MB) |
| TLS | memsz 0x318c4 (~203 KB) | `.tdata` (0x300) + `.tbss` |
- `init_array`: 27 entries (216 bytes); `fini_array`: 2; flags `BIND_NOW`; SONAME `libp2re.so`; no entry point (normal shared object). `GNU_EH_FRAME` present (C++ exceptions used; libc++ is **statically linked**, namespace `std::__ndk1`)

**Relocations (small).** `.rela.dyn` 751 entries (467 `ABS64`, 247 `GLOB_DAT`, **37 `TLSDESC`**), `.rela.plt` 256 `JUMP_SLOT`, plus **`.relr.dyn`** (Android/lld packed relative relocations, 0x5238 bytes = 2,631 words; `readelf` didn't decode them, so the count of relative relocs is unknown but is bounded by 63 × 2,631 ≈ 166k). The code itself is overwhelmingly PC-relative, so relocation work for a loader or converter is modest.

**Imports (257, all from the seven NEEDED libs).** Note `nm` prints a version suffix (`@LIBC`, `@LIBC_N`, `@LIBC_P`); the v1 import grep silently matched nothing because of it (fixed in v2 workflow for future runs).
- **NDK/platform (≈55):** `AAsset*` (8: asset reading, dir listing), `AAudio*` (10: stream builder, data callback, start), `AConfiguration*` (5), `AInputQueue*`/`AInputEvent*`/`AKeyEvent*`/`AMotionEvent*` (≈17), `ALooper_{prepare,pollOnce,addFd}`, `ANativeActivity_{finish,setWindowFlags}`, `ANativeWindow_{acquire,getWidth,getHeight,lock,setBuffersGeometry,unlockAndPost}`, `__android_log_print`. Note `ANativeWindow_lock`/`unlockAndPost` are **CPU-side** window drawing (used for message screens)
- **Standard libc/libm/pthread/stdio/wide-char/time:** the large remainder; mostly available on Darwin
- **Bionic-specific or awkward on Darwin:** `__sF` (Bionic stdio `FILE` array), `__errno`, fortify `__*_chk` (`__strlen_chk`, `__vsnprintf_chk`, `__read_chk`, `__pread_chk`, `__pwrite_chk`, `__write_chk`, `__fwrite_chk`, `__strchr_chk`, `__strrchr_chk`, `__open_2`), `__system_property_get`, `__cxa_thread_atexit_impl`, `__register_atfork`, `gettid`, `pthread_getcpuclockid`, `sched_setaffinity`, `__sched_cpucount`, `timer_create`/`timer_settime` (not on Darwin), `syscall`, `getauxval`, `dl_iterate_phdr`, `dladdr`/`dlopen`/`dlsym`, `arc4random`, `android_set_abort_message`, `openlog`/`syslog`/`closelog`, `setpriority`, `_setjmp`/`_longjmp`/`setjmp`/`longjmp`, `sigaction`, `mmap`/`mprotect`/`munmap`/`madvise`, `posix_memalign`/`aligned_alloc`
- **Struct-layout hazards:** Bionic `stat`, `dirent`, `FILE`, `pthread_mutex_t`/`cond_t`/`rwlock_t`/`attr`, `sigaction`, `timespec`/`itimerspec` differ from Darwin; any import taking or returning them needs a **marshalling shim**
- No imports of `libvulkan`, GLES, EGL, OpenSL (Vulkan is `dlopen`ed at runtime)

**Exports (82,143).** 80,665 × `f_<hexaddr>(Cpu*)`; 64 × `f_<hexaddr>_lifted(Cpu*)`; 1,478 others (~442 are libc++ symbols). Of the 80,727 sized recompiled functions: min 20 B, median 484 B, max 366,716 B. Non-libc++ exports reveal the architecture: `ANativeActivity_onCreate` (entry), `SetNativeWindow(ANativeWindow*,int,int)`, `HaveNativeWindow`, `StartGuest(string,string)`, `LoadModules`, `InitGuestModules`, `CheckGameFiles`, `ReadBundledFile`, `RegisterApi`/`RegisterApiThunk`/`ApiThunkFor`/`ApiThunkByName` (Win32 API thunks by DLL+name), `BuildVtable`/`ComMethod`/`ComRelease`/`ComNotImpl`/`CreateXAudio2`/`IsXAudio2Clsid` (COM objects), `GuestAlloc`/`GuestRealloc`/`GuestVirtualAlloc`/`Free`/`Query`, `RecompGuestBase`, `HostDispatch`/`HostUnknownTarget`/`LookupGuestFunction`/`Dispatch`/`CallGuest`/`RegisterGuestOverride`, `FiberMain`/`FiberSwitch`/`FiberTrampoline`, `RecompLongjmp`, `DoubleToExtended`/`ExtendedToDouble` (x87 80-bit), `HostLockAtomics`/`HostUnlockAtomics`, `PollGameState`, **a full touch-control overlay** (`TouchJoystick`, `TouchButtons`, `TouchEditMode`, `TouchCameraGain`, `SetTouchMouse`, …), `FrameGenOn`/`NgfxSetFrameGen` (`ngfx::fg::Frame`: **frame generation**), `AdpfRegisterThread` (Android performance hints), `DrawMessageScreen(ANativeWindow*, …)`, `VkpddiHostWindow`.

**Guest memory model (important).** Disassembly of sampled recompiled functions shows every guest load/store as `host_ptr = (uint32 guest_addr) | 0x2000000000` (`orr xN, xN, #0x2000000000`). So **a 4 GB guest window must be mapped at the fixed host address `0x2000000000` (2^37 = 128 GB)**. Matching strings: "cannot reserve the 4 GB guest window at its fixed address (in use)", "guest window at %p", "cannot make the guest window writable", `g_guestBase`. Guest state is a `Cpu` struct (x86 registers at small offsets with `esp` at +16, flag bytes near +512…523, and an **indirect-call cache table at +8768** of 16-byte {guest addr, host function pointer} entries; a miss calls `Dispatch(Cpu*, u32)`). **Ahead-of-time only: no dynarec/JIT evidence.** The game does not need JIT; only *our loader* might (see §6a). Threading: "onecore: all guest threads pinned to cpu %d", guest threads are fibers (`CreateFiber … guest stack %u KB`), and **guest exception dispatch is not supported** (`RaiseException … continuing`).

**Game data requirement.** The APK carries lifted *code*; it reads the user's PC game folder at runtime. Required files (strings): `prototype2.exe`, **`prototype2engine.dll` (exactly 17,336,320 bytes; version-checked)**, `binkw32.dll`, and `.rcf` archives (`art`, `00audio`, `01audio`, `boot`, `cells`, `enable_dlc`, `movies`, `scripts`, `shaders`, `story_a_to_h`, `story_i_to_z`, `woi`). `msvcr90.dll` is bundled in `assets/`. Default search paths: `/storage/emulated/0/Prototype2/game`, `/storage/emulated/0/Prototype2`, `/storage/emulated/0/Prototype 2`, `/sdcard/p2`; `<externalFilesDir>/game_folder.txt` set by FolderPicker. Other files: `controls.cfg`, `pipelines.bin`, `vkpddi_cache`, `ngfx_diag`. Android-GPU-specific sysfs reads (`/sys/class/kgsl/kgsl-3d0/gpubusy`, `cpufreq/cpuinfo_max_freq`) must be tolerated when absent.

**Shared framework.** A log string reads **"Far Cry 3 recompiled (Android): %u guest functions, game data in %s"**, and earlier `NFSRE_VULKAN`. So this is a **multi-game static-recompilation runtime** (shared platform/D3D9/XAudio2 layers); Prototype 2 is one target. Implication: one team likely maintains it, and an iOS port of the runtime could serve other titles.

**Renderer.** `ngfx` (Vulkan wrapper) + `vkpddi` (D3D9 device). ~190 Vulkan entry points requested, notably: dynamic rendering (`vkCmdBeginRendering`/`EndRendering`), sync2 (`vkCmdPipelineBarrier2`, `vkCmdWriteTimestamp2`), extended dynamic state 1 and 2 (cull mode, front face, topology, depth/stencil enables and ops, `…WithCount`, `vkCmdBindVertexBuffers2`, rasterizer-discard, depth-bias-enable, primitive-restart), `vkCmdBlitImage2`, timeline semaphores (`vkWaitSemaphores`, `vkGetSemaphoreCounterValue`), legacy render passes/framebuffers (also), `vkCmdPushDescriptorSetKHR`, query pools, compute dispatch, `vkCreateAndroidSurfaceKHR`. Extension names present in strings: `VK_KHR_swapchain`, `VK_KHR_surface`, `VK_KHR_android_surface`, `VK_KHR_maintenance5`, `VK_KHR_maintenance6`, `VK_KHR_load_store_op_none`/`VK_EXT_load_store_op_none`, `VK_EXT_custom_border_color`, `VK_EXT_depth_clip_enable`, `VK_EXT_robustness2`, `VK_EXT_debug_utils`, `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation`. The log line "ngfx: device ready (… custom border colors %d, f32 denorm flush %d preserve %d, nan preserve %d)" suggests custom border colors are **optional**. Texture handling includes BC→ASTC/RGBA8 conversion (`ngfx::AstcAsRgba8`, `BcConvertedSize`), which suits iPhones (no native BC).

**Shaders.** `assets/spirv/` = 25 MB: 473 `*.ps.spv`, 183 `*.vs.spv`, 112 `*.ps.low.spv`, 1 `*.ps.light.spv`, 1 `flat.ps.spv`. A 110-shader sample: **SPIR-V 1.3, only `OpCapability Shader`, `Logical GLSL450`**. Plain shaders; SPIRV-Cross (inside MoltenVK) should handle these.

**MoltenVK coverage (web-searched 2026-10-31).** MoltenVK **1.3.0 (2 May 2025) added Vulkan 1.3**, including `VK_KHR_maintenance6` and `VK_KHR_load_store_op_none`/`VK_EXT_load_store_op_none`. **1.4.0 (20 Aug 2025) added Vulkan 1.4** and `VK_KHR_maintenance5`. `VK_EXT_robustness2` has been supported since 1.0.44; `VK_KHR_push_descriptor` and `VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering` appear in older release extension lists. **Not confirmed by that search:** `VK_EXT_custom_border_color` and `VK_EXT_depth_clip_enable` support, exact behavior of float-controls (denorm/NaN) queries, and whether a MoltenVK newer than 1.4.0 exists today. Verify against the current MoltenVK release notes/extension list before relying on them.

### Java side
- Only one class decompiled: `com.p2re.prototype2.FolderPicker` (an Activity)
  - Launches `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT_TREE`, converts the chosen tree URI to a filesystem path (handles `primary` and `/storage/<id>`), and writes it to `<externalFilesDir>/game_folder.txt`
  - So the native code reads the user-supplied game folder path from that text file and then accesses the game files by plain filesystem paths (this is why `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` is requested)

### Interesting strings in `libp2re.so`
- Renderer ("ngfx") is **Vulkan 1.3 only**: "ngfx: Vulkan 1.3 required", "No Vulkan graphics driver", "Vulkan 1.3 (this driver has %u.%u)"
- User-facing error text: "Prototype 2 needs a GPU with a Vulkan 1.3 driver: Adreno 6xx or newer (Snapdragon) or a recent Mali / Immortalis (MediaTek Dimensity, Google Tensor, Exynos)…"
- `vkpddi: null D3D9 device (replace mode), screen %ux%u`, `vkpddi (Vulkan)`, `vkpddi: no Vulkan 1.3 device`. Suggests a **D3D9 device implementation backed by Vulkan** ("replace mode" is not understood yet)
- `XAudio2: AAudio output started (%d Hz)`, `XAudio2: AAudio unavailable`. **XAudio2 implemented on top of AAudio**
- `XInputGetState`, `XInputSetState`, `XInputGetCapabilities`, `XINPUT9_1_0`, `XINPUT1_3`. **XInput shim**
- `binkw32.dll` (Bink video DLL name referenced), `_CIfmod` (an MSVC x87 CRT helper symbol), `scripts/init.lua`
- Environment-variable-like name: **`NFSRE_VULKAN`**. Suggests the runtime is shared with a sibling project named "NFS RE" (likely a *Need for Speed* recompilation)
- **Function naming:** `_Z10f_105d3d90P3Cpu`, `_Z10f_103d3d90P3Cpu`, `_Z10f_100d3d90P3Cpu`, which demangle to **`f_<hex address>(Cpu*)`**. A function per original x86 address, each taking a CPU-state struct pointer

### Shaders
- `assets/spirv/` contains many **precompiled SPIR-V** pixel shaders, named by a 64-bit hash, with variants `*.ps.spv`, `*.ps.light.spv`, `*.ps.low.spv` (low-quality variants). Top-60 listing only showed pixel shaders (`.ps`); vertex shaders may exist but were not in the top 60 by size

### What this means for the 70 "missing Android functions" (user statement, Turn 22)

**[USER-STATED, Turn 22, verbatim]:** "Yup and you do know the 70 android functions are the devs custom runtime right"

Reading, consistent with the dev's words and the import list **[INFERRED]**: the 70 (now 52 after the v13 shims) undefined imports (`ANativeWindow_*`, `AAsset*`/`AAssetManager_*`, `ALooper_*`, `AInputQueue_*`, `AInputEvent_*`/`AMotionEvent_*`/`AKeyEvent_*`, `AConfiguration_*`, `AAudio*`, `ANativeActivity_*`, plus Bionic extras) are **the platform layer of the dev's closed-source host/runtime** (the dev's "host": "the controls and etc and optimizations", "controls audio, etc", plus the from-scratch renderer). They are not part of the recompiled game code; the host calls them on Android, and **on iOS we must supply them ourselves** as the iOS side of that host. There is no upstream source to borrow (dev: "my host and etc are closed sourced"), and no one-tap path (dev: "there is no one tap port option"). Note the dev's statement that the game was translated "from psuedo code" (decompiler-style output), not lifted from x86 machine code as §4 first assumed.

## 4d. Dev runtime facts from `strings.txt` and the logs (Turn 27, **[CONFIRMED]** unless marked)

- `strings.txt` is in `apk-inspection-v2.zip` (artifact of `inspect-apk-v2.yml`; GitHub Actions run page > Artifacts, expires after ~90 days, re-runnable with the APK link).
- Visible UI/diagnostic text in the library: see §0 Turn 27 item 7. Debug/telemetry strings include `perf hud`, `hudlog`, `touchlog`, `synctrace`, `call counts: ... callcounts_%d.txt`, `call counting enabled`.
- GPU/driver sniffing in the runtime is Android-oriented (`Adreno (TM) %d`, `vulkan.adreno`, `mali_sim`, `kgsl gpubusy`) but the game accepted the Apple GPU (`native renderer supported`) and chose `graphics quality: VERY LOW (first launch default; mid-range GPU)`.
- Save/profile path inside the guest: `C:\Users\Player\Documents\Activision\Prototype 2\{profile.bin,slot-A..T.bin}` -> `Documents/Prototype2/game/drive_c/Users/Player/Documents/Activision/Prototype 2/`.
- Guest memory window in the Android build's own log: `guest window at 0x7800000000` (the handoff's older text says `0x2000000000`; the loader's design for the window address is covered in §7; both numbers appear, treat the runtime's own log as the current truth **[UNVERIFIED which constant is patched in our build]**).

## 5. Interpretation (inferred, **not** verified)

Treat each as a hypothesis to confirm with the next workflow.

1. **Static recompilation.** The `f_<address>(Cpu*)` naming, the `Cpu` struct, `_CIfmod`, `msvcr90.dll`, `binkw32.dll`, and 82k exported functions strongly suggest the original x86 PC executable was lifted/recompiled to C++ (one function per guest function) and compiled to ARM64. The result is **native ARM64 machine code**, not emulation and not a JIT.
2. **Win32 API shim layer.** The recompiled code calls Win32/D3D9/XAudio2/XInput APIs that are reimplemented in the runtime (D3D9→Vulkan via "ngfx"/"vkpddi", XAudio2→AAudio, XInput→Android input).
3. **Shaders precompiled to SPIR-V** (D3D9 shader bytecode → SPIR-V ahead of time), so no runtime shader translation is needed beyond what the Vulkan driver does.
4. **The runtime's contact surface with Android is small:** `libandroid` (NativeActivity, ANativeWindow, input, asset/looper APIs), `liblog`, `libaaudio`, `libdl`/`dlopen("libvulkan.so")`, plus libc/libm/libz. This is the good news: the thing to replace on iOS is narrow and well-defined.
5. **The asset `msvcr90.dll`** may be loaded as a PE image or used for CRT data. Role unknown.
6. **Guest memory model.** A recompiled 32-bit x86 game on a 64-bit host typically reserves a guest address space (often up to 4 GB virtual) and addresses it through a base pointer. This has implications for iOS memory limits (§7). Not verified.
7. **Not a normal "port".** The APK is a build of a project derived from the game's own code, which has legal implications (§10).

---

### 5a. How to explain the architecture to the user (Turn 27, **[INFERRED]** from logs + the dev's words)

Three layers inside `libp2re.so`: translated x86 game code (runs natively as ARM64), the dev's Windows-to-Vulkan layer (D3D9 `vkpddi`, Vulkan renderer `ngfx`, own XAudio2/DirectInput), and the thin Android edge (~70 NDK imports) that this project implements for iOS together with the MoltenVK bridge. This is **static recompilation**, not a source port: the user owns the iOS host side, the dev owns game logic and the Windows layer. The user's recurring worry ("per-frame mirroring costs CPU like emulation") does not apply: the shims run on events, not per instruction, and the measured per-frame cost outside `vkQueuePresent` is ~3 ms. The user wants a port that is "mine" without contacting the dev for every feature; the honest boundary is: everything on the iOS side and everything reachable through files, the Vulkan wrappers and our overlay is theirs; changing in-engine behaviour requires decompilation (a separate, very large project, tools to mention: Ghidra/IDA on the original exe/DLL; the N64 recomp community as reference). The user said they want to learn; explain terms: static recompilation, shim/HLE layer, ABI, Vulkan-on-Metal.

## 6. Strategy

### Why the original plan is dead
Decompiling Java and rewriting game logic in Metal is not applicable. There is **no game logic in the Java**, and `libp2re.so` is not decompilable to maintainable C++ at this scale (82k functions of recompiler output).

### New framing
"Port the port" and "wrap in Metal" are the **same plan** here: keep the recompiled ARM64 game code, replace the Android platform layer with iOS equivalents, and map Vulkan to Metal.

### Components needed on iOS
| Android piece used by the lib | iOS replacement |
|---|---|
| `NativeActivity` / `ANativeActivity_onCreate`, `ALooper`, `AInputQueue`, `ANativeWindow` | A custom shim exposing the same C API, backed by `UIApplicationDelegate` + `CAMetalLayer` + `UITouch`/`GCController` |
| `dlopen("libvulkan.so")` → Vulkan 1.3 | **MoltenVK** (Vulkan on Metal). **Unverified:** whether current MoltenVK covers every Vulkan 1.3 feature/extension this renderer requires. Check at the time |
| `libaaudio.so` | Shim to `AudioUnit` or `AVAudioEngine` |
| `liblog.so` | Shim to `os_log`/`NSLog` |
| `libc`/`libm`/`libdl` (Bionic) | Darwin libc (mostly compatible, but Bionic-specific symbols and TLS usage need checking) |
| `libz.so` | System zlib |
| `game_folder.txt` + `/storage/...` path | `UIDocumentPicker` with security-scoped bookmarks, then copy or reference the folder inside the app sandbox |
| `FolderPicker.java` | Replace entirely |

### The central problem: running ELF code on iOS
`libp2re.so` is an **ELF** shared object using Android's loader. iOS needs **Mach-O**, code-signed, with no writable+executable memory unless JIT is enabled.

- **Option A1: Offline ELF → Mach-O conversion (preferred to investigate).** Write a tool (runs in GitHub Actions) that takes the ARM64 ELF, applies/rewrites relocations, rebases data, handles `.init_array`/TLS/`eh_frame`, and emits a Mach-O dylib or static object, resolving imports to the shim layer. Code is then signed with the app, so **no JIT is needed**. Hard but conceptually clean. Since AArch64 code is mostly position-independent and the import surface is small, it may be feasible. TLS, exception unwinding, and relocation types are the likely traps.
- **Option A2: Runtime ELF loader.** Embed a custom ELF loader that maps segments with `mmap`, applies relocations, then runs. Requires executable pages at runtime, which on iOS means JIT enablement. **Since Turn 5 the user reports they can enable JIT**, so this is now realistic and is the **likely fastest route to a first boot**. Simpler to write than A1. Remaining work is the same shim layer plus: TLS setup, `.init_array`, C++ exception unwinding registration (`__register_frame`/eh_frame), Bionic-vs-Darwin libc symbol mapping (resolving the lib's imports to our shim table), and `__clear_cache`/icache handling.
- **Option B: Find upstream source.** If the runtime and recompiler are open source (`p2re`, possibly related to an `NFSRE` project), build the runtime for iOS directly from source and compile the recompiled code for iOS. This would remove most of A1/A2. **Check this first.**
- **Option C: Re-lift from the original PC executable** using the upstream recompiler targeting iOS. Only possible if the recompiler is public and the user supplies their own legally obtained game binary.

**Recommendation order (revised Turn 5):** B (ask the authors / find source) in parallel with **A2 (runtime loader, now enabled by JIT)** to get a first boot → A1 (offline conversion) later if a JIT-free build is wanted. Don't wait for B before starting the skeleton work (§11 step 4).

---

### 6a. Refined plan after the v2 analysis (Turn 7)

1. **Go/no-go: the 4 GB window at `0x2000000000`.** On the target iPhone, can an app reserve 4 GB at exactly `0x2000000000` (probe with `mmap`/`mach_vm_allocate` fixed-address `PROT_NONE`, then commit and touch a slice)? iOS limits user virtual address space and memory (jetsam); relevant mechanisms may include the `com.apple.developer.kernel.extended-virtual-addressing` and `com.apple.developer.kernel.increased-memory-limit` entitlements, **whose availability for sideloaded builds is UNVERIFIED**. If the fixed window cannot be reserved, the compiled code would need patching (every `orr …, #0x2000000000` site) or a different base, which changes this project from "port" to "binary rewriting". Build a **probe IPA first** (§11).
2. **Loading the code.** Because relocations are tiny and the code is position-independent, two feasible routes:
   - **A2 (runtime loader, uses the user's JIT):** reserve one contiguous region; map text (0 – 0x5b28f00) and the two RW segments at the **same relative offsets** (the data segment is at +0x5b2cf00, which is not 16 KB-aligned relative to text end, so map it at the containing page and copy to the exact offset); apply `ABS64`/`GLOB_DAT`/`JUMP_SLOT`/**RELR**/`TLSDESC` relocations; run `init_array`; supply `dl_iterate_phdr` and register `.eh_frame` so C++ exceptions unwind; resolve the 257 imports to a **shim table** (Darwin libc where compatible, wrappers where not)
   - **A1-lite (JIT-free):** build a signed Mach-O dylib whose `__TEXT` contains the read-only/executable segment bytes at identical offsets, plus a zero-fill RW segment placed so the same relative layout holds; the loader then only copies and relocates the data. Worth it later if JIT proves fragile
3. **TLS (37 `TLSDESC` relocs, 203 KB block).** Implement the TLS descriptors with our own resolver that returns a per-thread block offset; every thread that runs guest/host code (including ones created through the shimmed `pthread_create`) must get a block.
4. **Platform shim layer (iOS side, the real work):** `ANativeActivity`/`ALooper`/`AInputQueue`/`AMotionEvent`/`AKeyEvent` event plumbing (UIKit touches + GameController), `ANativeWindow` (backed by a `CAMetalLayer`; plus the CPU `lock/unlockAndPost` path for message screens), `AAudio` → AudioUnit, `AAssetManager` (bundled `msvcr90.dll` and SPIR-V in the app bundle), `AConfiguration` (locale), `__android_log_print`, `dlopen("libvulkan.so")` → MoltenVK, Bionic libc quirks (§4b).
5. **Game data on iOS:** the user's PC folder (≈ several GB of `.rcf` archives) must be reachable via sandbox paths (copy into the app container or use security-scoped bookmarks); the existing path-detection must be redirected.
6. **Don't forget:** guest exceptions unsupported; GPU sysfs reads must fail gracefully; "onecore" thread pinning uses `sched_setaffinity` (not available on iOS; no-op shim).

---

## 7. Risks and unknowns

- **Fixed 4 GB guest window at `0x2000000000`** (§4b, §6a): may be impossible or require special entitlements on iOS; must be tested on-device before any other investment
- **Executing ELF-derived code on iOS** (above). Large but now well-bounded: tiny relocation tables and import surface
- **Vulkan 1.3 on Metal.** The renderer *requires* Vulkan 1.3; MoltenVK coverage and performance are unverified here. iPhone GPU/Metal feature level also matters (device unknown)
- **Memory limits / jetsam.** If the runtime reserves a large guest address space and the game uses 1–2+ GB, iOS may kill the app. Might need the increased-memory-limit entitlement (not available to all signing methods)
- **JIT / signing / install route** unresolved (SideStore vs TrollStore vs paid account), and depends on iOS version and device
- **Thread pointer / stack canary (Turn 13, [INFERRED]):** Android-built code may read `TPIDR_EL0` directly (TLS, stack protector). On iOS this register is not the system thread pointer; first executed guest code (even `__cxa_demangle`) may fault. Count and context are in the v6 report.
- **Possible hidden dependencies:** `msvcr90.dll` role, `binkw32.dll` (video playback may be stubbed or implemented), Lua scripts, any self-modifying code or runtime code generation (check for `mmap`/`mprotect` with `PROT_EXEC` in imports)
- **Performance claims are unverified.** Native ARM64 recompiled code should beat emulation, but how the phone's GPU handles the renderer through MoltenVK is unknown
- **Scope.** Even in the best case this is a large engineering effort. Be honest with the user about timelines

## 9. Corrections to the earlier (Qwen) plan. Don't repeat these

- "Hopper Disassembler iOS app": **no iOS version exists** (Hopper is desktop). Don't recommend it
- "ByteCode Viewer / iZip / File Organizer" apps: unverified or likely hallucinated; don't rely on them
- `java -jar dex2jar-1.0.jar input.dex output.class`: wrong syntax (dex2jar outputs a `.jar`). Moot here anyway; use jadx
- "`.so` files are likely Objective-C++": **wrong.** They are ARM64 ELF binaries built against Android's NDK, which iOS cannot load
- "Decompile `.so` to C++ source": not realistic. Decompilers give pseudo-code at best
- "JD-GUI in Safari via iframe hacks": nonsense; JD-GUI is a desktop Java app and doesn't handle DEX
- "Upload `.class/.so/.cpp` to GitHub" and "`clang --target arm64-apple-ios15.0`" workflow steps: not meaningful for this problem
- "Xcode Cloud via GitHub Actions": conflation. These are separate services

## 10. Legal and practical notes

- `libp2re.so` contains game code derived from copyrighted software, even though game *assets* aren't included. Redistribution is a real risk.
- Recommend: keep the APK, any derived binaries, and the game files **out of any public repo**. Prefer a private repo and personal use. The user's README links to a hosted APK, so mention the exposure plainly and without lecturing.
- The user must supply their own legitimately obtained game files.

### Turn 16: v8 (ran in Turn 17: only 5/613 patched, see Turn 17) = `build-loader-v8.yml`
**What changed vs v7 (only `prep.py` and a few lines of `p2core.c`):**
1. **613 `mrs xN,tpidr_el0` sites -> `b stub`**; stub = `adrp xN,blk; add xN,xN,#lo12; b site+4; nop` (16 bytes). `blk` = 64-byte **read-only** block in the code-page tail with constant canary `0x2A7F3C91B5D8E600` at +0x28. Single global canary is fine for stack-protector semantics.
2. **Per-site safety scan (conservative):** function bounds from sized dynsym entries; each function is disassembled once with `llvm-objdump` (4 threads); after the `mrs`, xN (or wN) may only appear as `ldr xT,[xN,#0x28]`; a `ldr xN,[xN,#0x28]` or a later `mrs xN,TPIDR_EL0` ends the scan. **Anything else (e.g. `add x0,x20,#0x10` TLS-style use, stores, moves) REFUSES the site**, it stays an unpatched `mrs` and will fault at 0x1004+off on the device (crash handler prints pc as elf+offset). No disassembler or no function bounds = refuse. Report section "thread pointer" prints patched/refused counts, free bytes left, a histogram of the offending mnemonics, and the first 25 refused sites with the offending instruction.
3. **Phantom-`ands` mask fixed** in the report line (see Turn 16 item 6); no change to patch counts.
4. App: after the TPIDR probe it logs the canary block value at +0x28 (expect `OK`). `layout.h` gains `P2_CANARY_BLK` / `P2_CANARY_VAL`.
5. Space: 613 x 16 + 64 = 9,872 bytes + 160 (v7 caves) of 12,544 free; asserted in the build (build fails loudly if it does not fit). Branch range ~85 MB < 128 MB (asserted per site).
**Tested [CONFIRMED in sandbox]:** helper unit tests (independent decode of every stub word: adrp page, add offset, return address, nop); scan cases (x19 vs x190 not confused, w-register mentions counted, kill rule); end-to-end `prep.py` on a synthetic aarch64 ELF with a fake objdump (3 of 5 sites patched, 2 correctly refused, branch words decode to the stubs, layout.h correct, YAML parses, `bash -n` on every run step, `gcc -fsyntax-only` on p2core.c). **NOT tested:** real llvm-objdump output parsing (parser written against the exact line format seen in the v7 report), the real library, iOS compile, on-device run. If the refused count is large, read the histogram before changing the rule.
**User steps:** add `build-loader-v8.yml` (private repo), run it, send back the Step summary (look at: "thread pointer" section, patched SHA) and the app's Copy report (look for `canary block ... OK` and the demangle lines).
Next after v8: compat shims, TLSDESC resolver, `.init_array`, `.eh_frame`, first boot (Turn 14 list).

### Turn 14: from smoke test to first boot (planned; step 1 done in Turn 15; steps 2-5 still open)
1. **Read the v6 extra scan** (needs the user's Step summary) and decide extra patch rules (`ubfm #37` window checks, `#0x21` end constants) before building on this binary. Patched SHA must be re-recorded if rules change.
2. **v7 = loader with a compat shim layer** (C, compiled into the test app, symbols resolved from it BEFORE `dlsym`): (a) the 19 Bionic-only functions; (b) **translating wrappers for flag/struct-sensitive imports** (`mmap`/`munmap`/`madvise`, `open`/`__open_2`/`*_chk`, `clock_gettime`, `sysconf`, `stat`/`fstat`/`opendir`/`readdir`, `pthread_*` incl. zero-init mutex/cond/rwlock, `setjmp` family, `sigaction`, `__sF`/stdio, errno mapping, `strerror_r`); (c) the 51 NDK imports as **logging stubs first** (log name + args, return 0/NULL), then real implementations in order: `__android_log_print` → `AAsset*`/`AConfiguration` (app bundle) → `ALooper`/`AInputQueue` → `ANativeWindow` (CAMetalLayer) → `AAudio`.
3. **TLS**: implement the 37 TLSDESC relocs (skipped now) and decide how code reaching `TPIDR_EL0` is handled (count is in the v6 report).
4. **Run `.init_array` (27 entries)**, register `.eh_frame`, supply `dl_iterate_phdr`. Add export offsets to `layout.h` (`ANativeActivity_onCreate`, `StartGuest`, `CheckGameFiles`, `RecompGuestBase`, `DrawMessageScreen`, `SetNativeWindow`).
5. **Milestone:** reserve the 4 GB window at `0x7800000000` (done by the runtime's own patched code; check it logs "guest window at %p"), then reach the runtime's own "game files missing" message screen (`DrawMessageScreen`). Only then: game-data plumbing, Vulkan→MoltenVK.
