If it succeeds, download artifact `p2-loader-ipa` (contains patched game code; delete after use), sideload it, tap **Run loader test**, then **Copy report** and paste it back.

Patch rules are added to `prep.py` only after looking at real context.

### Turn 9: JIT-free plan (supersedes the A2-first ordering)
1. **`scan-apk-v3.yml`** (written, not run): reports where relocations land (code vs data), DF_TEXTREL, the exact count of `orr xd,xn,#0x2000000000` guest-base sites in `.text`, other literal uses of the base, and the segment gap. If no relocation touches the executable segment, the 84 MB code can be embedded **unmodified** in a signed Mach-O and only data is relocated at runtime (no JIT).
2. **Base address:** `0x7000000000` (bits 36-38, a contiguous run) is a valid AArch64 logical immediate, so each `orr …,#0x2000000000` can be rewritten in place to `#0x7000000000` (same size, only immr/imms change). Needs the site count from step 1 and a check that the runtime's own reserve code and `g_guestBase` are handled. **[INFERRED; not tested]**. Also test the entitled IPA at 0x2000000000.
3. **Mach-O image:** assemble the ELF's first LOAD (0–0x5b28f00) with `.incbin` into `__TEXT`, the RW segments into `__DATA` at the same relative offsets (gap noted in scan), link as a dylib/static lib on `macos-latest`; the loader applies RELA/RELR/GLOB_DAT/JUMP_SLOT/TLSDESC to the data segment, resolves 257 imports to a shim table, registers `.eh_frame`, runs `init_array`, and calls exports by ELF symbol offset. **[PLAN; untested]**
4. Then shims (§6a step 4) and Vulkan→MoltenVK.

### Turn 8: probe app (status: written, **never built or run**)
**What it is:** `build-probe.yml` (in `.github/workflows/`), run from the Actions tab. Steps: write sources, `brew install xcodegen`, `xcodegen generate`, unsigned `xcodebuild -sdk iphoneos`, zip IPAs, `brew install ldid` + `ldid -S` for the entitled variant (`continue-on-error`), upload artifact `p2-probe-ipas`.
**Two IPAs:** `plain` (no special entitlements) and `entitled` (`extended-virtual-addressing`, `increased-memory-limit`, `get-task-allow` stamped with ldid). Whether the sideload tool keeps those entitlements, or refuses to install the entitled one on a free account, is **[UNVERIFIED]**. Try plain first, then entitled, and compare.
**App behavior:** on launch (safe tests, auto): device model, iOS version, RAM, page size, `os_proc_available_memory`, rlimits, P_TRACED (debugger/JIT enabler attached); 64 MB map at hints 0x100000000 … 0x10000000000 to find the VA ceiling; **4 GB `PROT_NONE` reserve at `0x2000000000`** (falls back 3/2/1/0.5/0.25 GB), then `mprotect` RW on the whole region and touch 256 MB. Buttons (risky, may kill the app): **Memory ramp** (touch 256 MB steps up to 6 GB, log each step), **JIT test** (`MAP_JIT` RWX, run a `mov w0,#42; ret` stub), **W^X test** (RW map, `mprotect` RX, run stub, which is what a plain ELF loader needs). **Copy report** puts the log on the clipboard. The report is rewritten on every line to `Documents/probe_report.txt` (file sharing enabled, visible in Files) so it survives a kill.
**Reading results:** full 4 GB reserved at the fixed address = GO for the window; only smaller sizes or "not honored" = need to patch the `orr …, #0x2000000000` sites or pick another base (check the hint table for which bases work); memory-ramp last line = real jetsam ceiling; JIT/W^X lines say whether loader route A2 is viable with the user's JIT method.
**Known limits:** compile not verified (no iOS SDK in the sandbox); `pthread_jit_write_protect_np` is looked up with `dlsym` to avoid SDK availability errors; MAP_JIT is defined manually if missing.

### Revised (Turn 7), supersedes the list below where they conflict
1. **Build a probe IPA** (CI on `macos-latest`: generate an Xcode project with XcodeGen, `xcodebuild -sdk iphoneos CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO`, zip into an unsigned IPA artifact; the user signs/installs with their sideload tool). The probe app reports on screen/log: iOS version, device model, RAM, `os_proc_available_memory`, `getrlimit`; whether it can `mmap` **4 GB at `0x2000000000`** (reserve, then commit/touch 256 MB–1 GB), whether `MAP_JIT`/RWX mapping works with the user's JIT method, and whether a tiny hand-made AArch64 stub copied into an executable page can run. **This answers the go/no-go in §6a.**
2. Ask in the Discord (§8 Q1, Q7): source availability for the runtime/recompiler, license, and iOS interest.
3. Check the current MoltenVK release notes for `VK_EXT_custom_border_color`, `VK_EXT_depth_clip_enable`, float-controls behavior, and whether a release newer than 1.4.0 exists.
4. If go: build a **minimal loader** that loads `libp2re.so` into the probe app and calls an exported symbol that doesn't need the platform (e.g. a pure `f_<addr>` function), then add platform shims incrementally (log → memory → threads → input → audio → window/Vulkan).
5. Only then: game-data plumbing and a first "boot to message screen" (the runtime already draws a message screen via `DrawMessageScreen` when files are missing, a natural milestone).

### Original list (Turn 4, partly done)

1. **Ask the provenance question** (§8 Q1). If upstream source exists, switch to Option B.
2. ~~Run `inspect-apk-v2.yml`~~ **Done Turn 7** (results in §4b). It collected:
   - `readelf -lW` (program headers), `readelf -SW` (sections and sizes), `readelf -r` summary by relocation type (count `R_AARCH64_RELATIVE`, `GLOB_DAT`, `JUMP_SLOT`, `TLS*`, `IRELATIVE`)
   - `readelf -d` full dynamic section (flags, `INIT_ARRAY`, `SONAME`)
   - `nm -D --undefined-only`: full list of **imports** (libc, libandroid, aaudio, dl symbols). This defines the exact shim surface
   - `nm -D --defined-only` filtered: non-`f_*` exports (entry points like `ANativeActivity_onCreate`, `android_main`, runtime API names)
   - Count of `f_*` functions; sample of export name patterns
   - Whether `mmap`/`mprotect`/`__clear_cache` are imported (runtime code generation?)
   - TLS usage (`.tdata`/`.tbss`), `.eh_frame` presence and size, C++ exception usage (`__cxa_throw`, `_Unwind_*`)
   - `strings` hunt for: `ANativeActivity`, `AInputQueue`, `AAudio`, `vk[A-Z]` function-name list (which Vulkan functions/extensions are requested), `VK_KHR_`/`VK_EXT_` extension names, `game_folder.txt`, file paths, `dlopen`ed library names
   - `assets/` full file listing (counts by extension, including vertex shaders `*.vs.spv`)
   - Full `AndroidManifest.xml` decoded (via `aapt dump xmltree`)
3. **Check MoltenVK's current Vulkan 1.3 / extension support** against the Vulkan extension list from step 2 (use web search at that time).
4. **Prototype the iOS skeleton in CI:** an Xcode project that builds on `macos-latest`, creates a `CAMetalLayer`, initializes MoltenVK, and draws a clear color. Proves the build/sign/install pipeline before touching the game.
5. **Prototype ELF → Mach-O (or loader) on a trivial test `.so`**, then scale up to `libp2re.so`.
6. **Decide the install route** (SideStore/TrollStore/paid account) based on the iOS version.

| `handoff.md` | this file | living context for any agent |

### v1 workflow summary (for rebuilding if lost)
Ubuntu runner → `apt install binutils file unzip aapt` + `pip install gdown` → `gdown --fuzzy <url> -O game.apk` → `unzip` → download jadx 1.5.0 → `jadx -d jadx_out --no-res --show-bad-code apk/classes*.dex` → build `report.md` (ls, `aapt dump badging`, `find apk/lib`, biggest assets, per-`.so`: `file`, `readelf -d | grep NEEDED`, `nm -D` counts, JNI exports, filtered `strings`) → write to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` → upload artifact `apk-inspection`.

---

## 13. Maintenance protocol (for Claude and any agent)

- **Update this file every turn** from now on. Keep one consistent file (same name, same section order). Add a dated changelog entry; don't silently rewrite history.
- Mark each claim as **[CONFIRMED]** (from artifacts), **[INFERRED]**, or **[UNVERIFIED]**. Move items between sections as evidence arrives.
- When the user pastes new output, record the relevant raw excerpts in the appendix and update §4–7.
- Keep the TL;DR (§0) accurate; it is what a new agent reads first.
- **Write the updated `handoff-turnNN.md` in the SAME reply as the analysis of any new upload, every turn, without being asked and without offering it first.** (The user had to remind Claude in Turn 28.)
- **Say exactly what was tested where.** The Linux/x86 sandbox cannot run or compile the Apple-only code paths; label such tests "pure-logic, off-device" and never imply they cover memory layout, ABI behaviour on the phone, or iOS compilation.
- **On a silent log end, ask for the iOS `.ips` (Analytics Data) before writing more diagnostics.**
- Never state versions, MoltenVK support levels, iOS JIT/sideload behavior, or tool availability from memory. **Verify them at the time** (web search) and record the date.

- **2026-11-01, Turn 30 (v24 result):** v24 ran (`p2_loader_log_20261101-131546.txt`, `p2.log`, `p2-disasm.zip` with `xref.txt`). Arena = base `0x09429c10`, limit `0x09a29c10` (6 MiB). At the crash `top` = `0x09f3aa10` (past the limit) and the failing call's args were size 0x10 / align 0x10: an earlier ~5.25 MB request overflowed the arena, `top` is stored before the limit check and never rolled back, so the next tiny request returns NULL and the caller dereferences it. Found that the `sysconf` shim used glibc numbers while the guest uses bionic numbers (game calls `sysconf(97)` = CPU count, got -1). Wrote **v25** (sysconf fix + new default `disasm_ranges` for the arena init/reset code). Earlier entry:
- **2026-11-01, Turn 30 (earlier):** Read `p2-disasm_3.zip` (ranges `0x5242800-0x5243000`, `0x3fdb8e0-0x3fdba00`, `0x56dade0-0x56daf00`). Result: `f_10937940` ends at `0x524288c`; EAX = `w10` is stored at `0x5242860`; `w10` is zeroed at `0x5242670` on the over-limit branch, so the function returns NULL exactly when `aligned(aligned_top + size) > limit`. Arena struct lives at guest `0x11008568..0x11008578`. Cause of the arena being full/uninitialised still unknown. **v24 WRITTEN** (`build-loader-v24.yml`, patch of the user's re-uploaded v23; see TURN 30 ADDENDUM item 6).

## Appendix A: raw excerpts from `report.md` (build 1.0.2; 1.0.3 differences in §4a)

```
package: name='com.p2re.prototype2' versionCode='3' versionName='1.0.2' compileSdkVersion='35'
sdkVersion:'29'  targetSdkVersion:'35'
uses-permission: MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE; READ/WRITE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE (maxSdk 32)
launchable-activity: android.app.NativeActivity
native-code: 'arm64-v8a'

lib/arm64-v8a/libp2re.so  97,013,032 bytes
ELF 64-bit LSB shared object, ARM aarch64, dynamically linked, stripped
BuildID[sha1]=39eb5891bdf27c9e59710180d073e384b88f0b78
NEEDED: libandroid.so liblog.so libaaudio.so libdl.so libz.so libm.so libc.so
exported symbols: 82141   JNI (Java_*) exports: none

assets/msvcr90.dll 656,272 bytes
```

## TURN 29 ADDENDUM (disasm_2 result, v23 re-run)

- Crash site recap: f_10963750 (elf+0x5366640) calls `bl f_10937940` at elf+0x5367108, reloads EAX from Cpu+0, pushes it, then does an x86 atomic xchg on [EAX] -> `ldar w20,[x21]` at elf+0x5367168 faults because EAX==0 (guest page 0 is a deliberate PROT_NONE guard).
- f_10937940 (elf+0x52424b0, guest 0x10937940, prototype2engine.dll) is NOT a getter. It is an allocator-like routine on a global at guest 0x11008574 (x20 = 0x2011008574 in disasm):
  * `ldurb w8,[x20,#-0xe]; cbz` -> branch at 0x5242600 (init/flag check).
  * Calls f_106ba320 (elf 0x3fdb8e0) via direct bl or Dispatch(0x106ba320).
  * 0x5242600: aligned bump: top=[x20]; align=w8; new=(align-1+size+aligned(top)) & -align; limit=[x20-4]; store new to [x20]; if new<=limit -> 0x5242698 (fast path, calls f_109ee80c elf 0x56dade0), else -> 0x5242860 (GROWTH PATH, not yet disassembled).
  * Other fields touched: [x20-0xc], [x20-0x10], [x20+4].
- Hypothesis: on iOS the growth path (or the callee f_106ba320 / f_109ee80c) fails and the routine returns NULL, and the caller never checks. Candidates: a VirtualAlloc/HeapAlloc-like shim returning failure, or uninitialised global pool.
- x18 lead is dead (only save/restore pair at 5b27c38/5b27ce0).
- Dispatch (elf 0x59ce560) disassembled; hash multiplier 0x9e3779b1, 64 probes (as before).

## TURN 30 ADDENDUM (disasm_3 result)

**Source [CONFIRMED]:** `p2-disasm_3.zip` / `disasm.txt`, `llvm-objdump` on the untouched `libp2re.so`. Conclusions below are hand-read from that listing unless marked.

**1. What `f_10937940(Cpu*)` is [CONFIRMED listing, INFERRED naming].** A bump/arena allocator over a static struct at guest `0x11008574` (`x20` = that address). Fields (offsets from `x20`): `[-4]` = **limit**, `[0]` = **top (cursor)**, `[-0xc]` = high-water mark, `[+4]` = a peak/second counter, bytes `[-0xe]`, `[-0xf]`, `[-0x10]` = flags (`-0xe` enables a tracking call to `f_106ba320`, a bitmap/hash test; `-0xf` enables a tracking call to `f_109ee80c`, which is only a small cache lookup that falls back to `Dispatch`). Arguments are on the guest stack: `size = [esp+4]`, `align = [esp+8]` after the prologue adjustments [INFERRED from register use].
**2. The NULL [CONFIRMED]:** `aligned_top = (top + align-1) & -align`; `new = (aligned_top + size + align-1) & -align`; `[top] = new` is stored BEFORE the check; `cmp new, limit; b.ls fast`. If `new > limit` the code sets `w10 = 0` (`0x5242670`) and branches to the epilogue `0x5242860`, which writes `w10` into guest EAX (`stp w10,w9,[x19]`). Otherwise EAX = `aligned_top`. **So EAX = 0 means the arena was out of space, or never set up (limit 0, top 0).** No import/shim is called on either path.
**3. What this rules out [CONFIRMED]:** a shim returning 0 inside `f_10937940`; the 4 traced memory calls (v23 ring was empty). **What it does not rule out:** the arena was sized wrongly earlier because some shim returned a wrong VALUE (memory size, page size, CPU count, time) to the code that initialises the arena; the arena init never ran or ran with a failed backing allocation.
**4. Callees [CONFIRMED listing]:** `f_106ba320` = tests bit `(([esp])>>2) % 10144` in a bitmap at guest `0x10fb04a0` and sets it if clear (tracking/dedup); `f_109ee80c` = looks up guest `0x10a462bc` in a small table in the Cpu struct, else `Dispatch`. Neither can set EAX.
**5. The game ran 60 fps for a long time before this [CONFIRMED earlier turns]**, so the arena probably worked earlier. Likely stories [INFERRED, none tested]: (a) this allocation is big (level/new-game setup at `state 6`) and the arena limit is too small because it was sized from a value our shims returned; (b) the arena is reset per phase and a reset is skipped; (c) the arena really is meant to run out and the caller should check, as on Windows with more memory.

### v24 RESULT (Turn 30) [CONFIRMED from `p2_loader_log_20261101-131546.txt` (header `== P2 loader v24 ==`), `p2.log`, `p2-disasm.zip` (`xref.txt`)]
**7. Same crash, same place** (`sig=10 fault=0x7800000000 pc=elf+0x5367168 lr=elf+0x536710c`). Failed-call ring still empty (0 of 0).
**8. The arena [CONFIRMED values, INFERRED field names]:** guest dwords at crash: `0x11008560 = 09429c10` (arena base), `0x11008564 = 00000001` (flags), `0x11008568 = 099faa00` (high-water), `0x1100856c = 2`, `0x11008570 = 09a29c10` (LIMIT), `0x11008574 = 09f3aa10` (TOP), `0x11008578 = 099faa00`. **Limit minus base = 0x600000 = 6 MiB.** Every 2-s `ARENA` line before the crash shows top = base (arena empty/reset), hwm = base, limit `09a29c10`, so the arena was never used until the new-game burst. At the crash: last successful top (hwm) = `099faa00` (about 6.1 MB used of 6.29 MB), then `top` = `09f3aa10`, which is `0x540010` (about 5.25 MB) past that, i.e. **an earlier request of about 5.25 MB did not fit and returned NULL** [INFERRED size].
**9. Why the crash is a tiny allocation [CONFIRMED stack/regs, INFERRED reading]:** Cpu words c1 = `fffffff0` (-align), c2 = `00000010` (align); guest stack at ESP-0xc..ESP-4 holds the return address `10963893` then `00000010`, `00000010` (size 0x10, align 0x10); ESP = `037fee5c` = after the stdcall pop of 8 bytes. So **the failing call asked for 16 bytes**. It failed because `f_10937940` stores `top = new` BEFORE comparing with the limit and never rolls back, so once the arena has overflowed, EVERY later request fails until the arena is reset. The caller does not check for NULL. **Open: why the arena overflowed on iOS (Android behaviour never observed here).** Husk gave no reference run.
**10. xref result [CONFIRMED listing]:** functions that build guest addresses in `0x11008560..0x1100857f`: `f_10937890` (elf+0x524dbd0, writes 0x...560 = base), `f_10937900` (elf+0x524dd94, 0x...560), `f_10937940` (the allocator), `f_10937ab0`/`f_109379f0`/`f_10937a30`/`f_10937aa0` (0x...56c), `f_10937ba0`/`f_10937ae0`/`f_10937b10`/`f_10937b60` (0x...566 flag byte), `f_10937dc0` (0x...57c), and `f_106b72a0`, `f_106b77f0`, `f_10763050`, `f_10763280` (0x...564 flags). The setter of the limit (0x...570) is not in the list (it is probably computed from the base with an offset), so `f_10937890`/`f_10937900` are the first to read.
**11. sysconf shim bug [CONFIRMED: log lines `PLAT sysconf(97) -> -1`; bionic numbers CONFIRMED by web search 2026-11-01 of the Android bionic `bits/sysconf.h` (android.googlesource.com): `_SC_PAGESIZE 0x27`, `_SC_NPROCESSORS_CONF 0x60`, `_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN 0x61`, `_SC_PHYS_PAGES 0x62`, `_SC_AVPHYS_PAGES 0x63`]:** the v14..v24 shim used the GLIBC numbers (2, 30, 83-86), so the game's CPU-count query (97 = 0x61) got -1. Whether this changes the arena size or thread sizing is UNKNOWN. v25 maps the bionic numbers.

### p2-disasm RESULT (Turn 31) [CONFIRMED listing `disasm.txt`; decode by hand from the objdump text, no emulator]
**Files.** `disasm.txt` has the six ranges from Turn 30 item 20. `xref.txt` (16 hits for guest `0x11008560..7f`) and `x18.txt` (two lines, `ldp/stp x18,x19,[x0,#0x90]`) are identical to the Turn 29 copies [CONFIRMED by content], so nothing new there.
**Cpu struct layout [INFERRED, consistent across all ranges]:** `+0 EAX, +4 ECX, +8 EDX, +0xC EBX, +0x10 ESP, +0x14 EBP, +0x18 ESI, +0x1C EDI`, flag bytes at `+0x200/0x202/0x206/0x207/0x20b`, next-EIP scratch at `+0x284`. Guest address = `x | 0x2000000000` in the untouched ELF (patched builds use `0x7800000000`). Dispatch cache: table at `Cpu+0x2240`, 16-byte entries, index `(w1 ^ w1>>9) & 0xff`, tag at `+0`, host function pointer at `+8`; a miss calls `Dispatch(Cpu*, guest_addr)`.
**Chain mapped to functions [CONFIRMED symbols, return addresses match the THREADDUMP chain exactly]** (innermost first):
1. `usleep` <- `e+5a8b10c`: inside `HostDispatch(Cpu*, u32)` (starts `0x5a8ab80`), right after `blr x8` at `0x5a8b108` (x8 = `[x28+0x18]`, the host function for the import). So the guest called an imported host function that ends in `usleep` (our Sleep shim).
2. `e+59ce7e0`: inside `Dispatch`.
3. `e+4179f40`: **`f_106e9b20`** (starts `0x4179eb0`, 0x80 bytes). Whole function decoded: it is an import thunk, x86 equivalent `push [esp+4]` / `call dword ptr [0x10a461e0]` / `ret`. `0x10a461e0` is the import-table slot (built by `mov/movk` as `0x2010a461e0`). It copies the one argument, pushes the return address `0x106e9b2b`, puts the argument in EAX, calls whatever the slot holds, then pops the return address into `Cpu+0x284`. The slot was resolved to our Sleep shim [INFERRED from the usleep frame].
4. `e+556c210`: inside **`f_109ba470`** (starts `0x556bd40`, ends about `0x556c44c`), right after `bl f_109c1500` at `0x556c20c`. Before that call it pushes `w25` as the argument and the constant `w20+0x194` as return address. So `f_109c1500` (at `0x55b9f80`, not yet seen) is a one-argument wrapper, and it reaches the thunk in item 3 by a tail jump [INFERRED: the thunk's saved lr is `0x556c210`, so nothing sits between]. **`w25` = the Sleep argument, set in the part of `f_109ba470` before `0x556c140` that was not dumped [UNKNOWN value].** Just before the Sleep, `f_109ba470` calls `f_109ba3d0` (at `0x556c450`, not yet seen) at `0x556c174`.
5. `e+5623328`: inside **`f_109ccd40`** (starts `0x5622b50`), right after `bl f_109ba470` at `0x5623324`. About 0x700 bytes of this function (its head) were not dumped.
6. `e+425eeec`: inside **`f_10704510`** (`0x425ee10`), after `blr x8`. Decoded: a virtual call `this->[ESI-ish member at +0x18]->vtable[2]` (loads `[[ecx+0x18]]+8`), then tests a field `[esi+8] == 2`.
7. `e+4263b28`: inside **`f_10704cc0`** (`0x42639c0`), after `blr x8` at `0x4263b18`. This function contains a real loop (`0x4263a80..0x4263b54`): it walks a **singly linked list** (next pointer at node `+0x48`, stops on 0) and for each node makes a virtual call on `vtable[1]` (`[[node]+4]`). So it is a per-frame "update all registered objects" style loop [INFERRED].
8. `e+415d05c`, `e+4178ddc`, `e+3f55aac`, `e+41798ac`: callers above that, not dumped. `e+41798ac` also ends the earlier crash chain, so it is the same game thread [INFERRED].

(b) `HostDispatch` at `0x5a8b128` calls `clock_gettime(1, ...)` (bionic `CLOCK_MONOTONIC = 1`) but only after a byte flag at `[x19]` equals 1 (looks like the API-timing/profiling path, `g_apiTimeEnabled` is nearby) [INFERRED]. **Darwin has no clock id 1** (its `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` is 6; I did not look this up today, so [UNVERIFIED], verify before relying on it). Whether our `clock_gettime` shim maps the bionic ids is **not known from this turn's files**: check it, because any game timer built on it would never advance.
**What this does NOT show.** The Turn 30 reading ("stuck in a wait loop forever") is **not supported yet**. A function that does `Sleep(n)` once per call and is called once per frame by a list walker would show the same chain in every dump. Two readings fit equally:
 - **(A) per-frame yield [fits: looper polls about 59/s, render thread idle, game FPS overlay says 60]:** the main thread is running frames and sleeping inside each one. The game is simply at a screen that waits for something else (a streaming/loading thread, input, a network step). The idle worker threads then mean "no job was ever queued" or "their wake-up was lost".
 - **(B) one long wait loop:** `f_109ba470` loops `f_109ba3d0` + `Sleep(w25)` until a condition changes.
To tell (A) from (B) we need either the head of `f_109ba470` (loop structure and `w25`) or a runtime count of Sleep calls with argument and caller.

So the main thread's per-frame work is: `f_106e6910 -> f_106e4610 -> f_10704cc0 (per object) -> f_10704510 -> f_109ccd40 -> f_109ba470 -> Sleep(60)`.

### v27 WRITTEN (`build-loader-v27.yml`, patch of the user's v26) [YAML parses; the SLEEPTRACE aggregation code compiled and ran on x86 as a pure-logic test only; the Apple build and device behaviour are NOT tested]
`sh_usleep` and `sh_nanosleep` are put in the shim table before `dlsym` (the v26 table had no entry for them, so the game's calls went straight to Darwin). They record (caller as elf offset, duration in microseconds) in a 16-slot table and write one line per second: `SLEEPTRACE last second: [us=... e+... xN] ... (total calls so far N)`. Max 300 lines. A line appears only when a sleep call happens after the second changed, so no line at all means no sleeps. Default `disasm_ranges` = `0x556c260-0x556c450 0x5577ae0-0x5577c00 0x55779e0-0x5577ae0 0x455c520-0x455c600 0x29dc180-0x29dc280` (loop exits, `f_109ba340`, `f_109ba320`, `f_107652a0`, `f_103d2170`).

**What it is:** a static scan of `libp2re.so` strings against MoltenVK's extension table (161 entries parsed). It does NOT query the device, so it says nothing about features, formats or behaviour at runtime. "Feature structs named (0)": none found.
**Extensions named in the binary that MoltenVK does NOT list (2):** `VK_EXT_custom_border_color` (optional for the game; the runtime logs `custom border colors %d`, and it logged 0), `VK_KHR_android_surface` (the loader already maps `vkCreateAndroidSurfaceKHR` to `vkCreateMetalSurfaceEXT`, log-confirmed).
**Named and listed by MoltenVK (9):** `VK_EXT_debug_utils`, `VK_EXT_depth_clip_enable`, `VK_EXT_load_store_op_none`, `VK_EXT_robustness2`, `VK_KHR_load_store_op_none`, `VK_KHR_maintenance5`, `VK_KHR_maintenance6`, `VK_KHR_surface`, `VK_KHR_swapchain`. Caveat: listing an extension is not the same as the device exposing its feature bit; the game's own GPU check (Turn 26 notes) logged `depth clip control 0` and `geometry shaders 0` on this phone.
**"geometry unsupported" is NOT in this report.** It comes from the game's startup line `BC textures 1, geometry shaders 0, depth clip control 0`.
**Entry points the renderer uses (162, all named):** dynamic rendering (`vkCmdBeginRendering/EndRendering`), many extended-dynamic-state setters (`CullMode`, `FrontFace`, `DepthTestEnable`, `DepthWriteEnable`, `DepthCompareOp`, `StencilOp`, `StencilTestEnable`, `PrimitiveTopology`, `DepthBiasEnable`, `RasterizerDiscardEnable`, `PrimitiveRestartEnable`, `*WithCount`), `vkCmdBindVertexBuffers2`, `vkCmdPushDescriptorSetKHR`, `vkCmdPipelineBarrier2`, `vkCmdWriteTimestamp2`, `vkCmdBlitImage2`, timeline semaphores (`vkWaitSemaphores`, `vkGetSemaphoreCounterValue`), compute (`vkCmdDispatch*`), indirect draws, queries. No tessellation or geometry-specific calls are visible by name.
**Relevance to the black 3D world [INFERRED, untested]:** every dynamic-state call the renderer makes is a place where MoltenVK could ignore or only partly honour a state without any error; the log shows no Vulkan errors, so a silent mismatch (culling, depth test/compare, depth bias, stencil, primitive restart) is possible. I did not check MoltenVK's per-state support today; verify against the current MoltenVK release notes before acting (a web search this turn returned only Vulkan 1.3/1.4 release notes, not a per-state table).

### MoltenVK facts used (web search 2026-11-01; **the docs snapshot I could read (skia mirror of MoltenVK `Docs/MoltenVK_Configuration_Parameters.md`, copyright 2015-2024) is OLDER than the 1.4.2 the project uses; names are probably unchanged but defaults/enum meanings are [UNVERIFIED for 1.4.2]**)
Settings can come from environment variables (read at instance creation; a `VK_EXT_layer_settings` entry would override them, our loader does not pass one). `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED`: 0 never, 1 always, 2 on demand (not for shaders asking for `SignedZeroInfNanPreserve`); documented default 1. `MVK_CONFIG_LOG_LEVEL`: 0 none, 1 errors, 2 +warnings, 3 +info (documented default 3), 4 +debug. `MVK_CONFIG_USE_METAL_ARGUMENT_BUFFERS` (boolean in that snapshot, documented default 1; newer releases may use an enum, 0 means off either way). `MVK_CONFIG_SHADER_DUMP_DIR`: dumps SPIR-V, converted MSL and pipeline shader lists to a directory. `MVK_CONFIG_TRACE_VULKAN_CALLS` exists but logs every call (far too much at 800 draws/frame), not used.

### v29 (`build-loader-v29.yml`, patch of the user's v28) [WHAT WAS TESTED, exactly]
Tested off-device on x86 (Linux, gcc, AddressSanitizer + UBSan): the new C block in isolation with a fake `L()`, a fake MoltenVK and a fake Documents folder (all checks passed): env switches and `P2_SWITCHES.txt`, the 32k-entry image/view table (30,000 inserts, lookup still correct, table-full path), the NONE-op rewrite (real call sees LOAD/STORE, caller's structs untouched, trace record keeps the ORIGINAL ops), per-thread draw counting (a draw on another thread counts as outside any pass), legacy pass record, `vktrace_now` folder AND file removal, automatic dump at present #900. Also: the YAML parses, `bash -n` passes on all 11 run steps, the whole assembled `p2core.c` has no error or warning inside the v29 block under `gcc -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra` (16 errors remain, all in older Apple-only code: `vm_read_overwrite`, `task_info`, `Dl_info`, mach types; they are Linux-only artefacts, as in earlier turns), and the CI strings script ran on a synthetic library. **NOT tested: the Apple/iOS compile, the real MoltenVK, the real game, any behaviour on the device.** The struct layouts used for `VkRenderingInfo`, `VkRenderingAttachmentInfo`, `VkRenderPassBeginInfo`, `VkImageCreateInfo`, `VkImageViewCreateInfo` are written from my memory of the Vulkan headers (offsets re-derived by hand: RenderingInfo.pColorAttachments at 48, AttachmentInfo.loadOp at 44 / clearValue at 52, RenderPassBeginInfo.clearValueCount at 48, ImageViewCreateInfo.format at 36, ImageCreateInfo.usage at 56); **a wrong offset would show up as nonsense formats/sizes in `VKTRACE` lines (or a crash), so check the numbers look sane before drawing conclusions.**

Changes in detail:
1. **Switches (folders or files with these exact names in `Documents`, create a New Folder in the Files app; read when the game loads Vulkan, so change them with the app closed; `P2_SWITCHES.txt` is rewritten each run and lists them):** `mvk_fastmath_on` (fast-math ON, MoltenVK's default), `mvk_fastmath_auto` (2), **default without any folder = fast-math OFF (the experiment)**; `mvk_argbuf_off`; `mvk_dumpshaders` (writes `Documents/mvk_shaders/`, big, short run only); `mvk_log_info` (MoltenVK log level 3, default 2); `vk_loadop_fix_off`; `vktrace_now` (runtime trigger, see 3).
2. **`mvk_stderr.log`:** stderr (fd 2) is redirected to `Documents/mvk_stderr.log` before MoltenVK loads, so MoltenVK's warnings/errors (shader conversion/compile failures, unsupported formats) become readable. `MVKENV ...` line in the loader log shows which settings were applied. `setenv` runs on the game thread inside the guest's `dlopen("libvulkan.so")`; a theoretical race with other threads calling `getenv` at that moment is accepted.
3. **VKTRACE:** wrappers (returned from `vkGetDeviceProcAddr`, `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` and guest `dlsym` on the Vulkan token) for `vkCmdBeginRendering/EndRendering` (+KHR), `vkCmdBeginRenderPass/EndRenderPass` (legacy; **RenderPass2 is NOT traced**), `vkCmdDraw`, `vkCmdDrawIndexed` (indirect draws are not counted), `vkCmdSetViewport/Scissor` (+`WithCount`, +EXT), `vkCmdSetCullMode`, `SetFrontFace`, `SetDepthTestEnable`, `SetDepthWriteEnable`, `SetDepthCompareOp` (+EXT), and `vkCreateImage/vkCreateImageView` (to learn the format and size behind each attachment). One record per render pass, stamped with the present counter. Dump format, one line per pass: `VKTRACE #seq dyn|legacy WxH draws=N | C0 fmtF WxH LOAD|CLEAR|DONT|NONE/STORE|DONT|NONE clr(r,g,b,a) | D fmtF WxH load/store z=Z | draw1 vp(x,y,w,h zmin-zmax) sc(x,y,w,h) cullC ffF dtT dwW opO | draw50 ...`. `fmt` is the Vulkan format number (97 RGBA16F, 100 R32F, 37 RGBA8, 44 BGRA8, 130 D32S8; -1 = image not in the table); `cull` 0 none/1 front/2 back/3 both, `ff` 0 CCW/1 CW, `op` is the VkCompareOp (1 LESS, 3 LESS_OR_EQUAL, 7 ALWAYS), -1 = that state was never set dynamically on the thread (it may be baked into the pipeline instead). `draw1`/`draw50` = state captured at the 1st and 50th draw of the pass. **When it prints:** automatically at present #900, #1800, ... (every 15 s at 60 fps, 20 times), and on demand: while the game runs, make a folder named `vktrace_now` (switch to Files, create it, switch back); at the next 30-frame boundary the loader prints a dump and deletes the folder, so it can be repeated. The dump shows the frame whose stamp is present-1 (or the newest stamp if none). Header counts: passes and draws so far and draws seen outside any traced pass (if that number grows fast and few passes print, the game starts passes with a call v29 does not trace).
4. **`LOAD_OP_NONE` / `STORE_OP_NONE` rewrite, ON by default** (`vk_loadop_fix_off` disables): for dynamic-rendering passes, attachments whose load op is `VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_NONE` (1000400000) become LOAD and store op NONE (1000301000) becomes STORE, on a stack copy (the game's structs are untouched; the trace records the original ops). Reason [INFERRED, untested]: the game uses `VK_KHR/EXT_load_store_op_none` (log: `load_store_op_none KHR 1 / EXT 1`); if MoltenVK treats NONE as "don't care", a depth or colour target keeps undefined content and every 3D draw can fail its depth test or blend over garbage while 2D (no depth) works. LOAD/STORE is a safe superset (costs bandwidth only). **Not covered:** legacy render passes (their ops live in the render pass object).
5. **CI step "Strings around the runtime's own debug switches":** from the untouched `libp2re.so`, prints for each marker name (`vkshot nogeometry hudlog synctrace touchlog watch cs_trust mali_sim dev_features debugoff waitspin yieldspin pinfast fake_aspect render_scale frame_gen_auto_off skip_file_check cmdline env controls.cfg game_folder.txt args.txt`) the exact-match hits and the 12 strings before/after in `.rodata` (neighbours often belong to the same function; not guaranteed), plus lists of screenshot/dump strings, the dev's log vocabulary (`graphics`, `vkpddi`, `ngfx`, `fg:`, `render`, `shader`, `effect`, `texture`, `depth` ...) and depth/black/blank strings. Output `disasm/markers.txt`, in the job log, the step summary (first 700 lines) and artifact `p2-disasm`. Only bytes before `.text` (offset `0x9c77a0`) are scanned.
6. The x18 and arena-xref CI steps are switched off with `if: false` (their results are in this file; the arena crash is fixed since v25); `disasm_ranges` default is now empty (the input still works).
7. Header `== P2 loader v29 ==`.

### How to read the result (decision guide) [INFERRED plan]
- **Does the world appear with the defaults?** Then bisect: create `mvk_fastmath_on`, run; then delete it and create `vk_loadop_fix_off`, run. Whichever brings the black back is the cause; record it.
- **No change:** read `VKTRACE` from a frame where the world should be visible. Questions: (a) Is there a pass with many draws (hundreds) writing a colour target of fmt 97 (or other) at the scene size, and what are its load op / clear colour (a clear colour of black and `STORE` is normal; `NONE` on depth would point at the rewrite theory)? (b) Does the same pass show `cull3` (both faces culled), `dt1` with `op` 0 (NEVER) or a viewport/scissor of zero size, or `z` range 0-0? (c) Is there a later pass (fmt 44 or the swapchain size, few draws) that should sample the scene target, and are its draws present? (d) Does `mvk_stderr.log` contain shader or pipeline errors? (e) If few passes are traced and `draws outside any traced pass` is large, the game uses a call v29 does not trace (add `vkCmdBeginRenderPass2`, indirect draws).
- Then `mvk_dumpshaders` for a short run to read the converted MSL of the 3D shaders (search for `fast_math`, `discard_fragment`, NaN-sensitive code), and the `markers.txt` strings to learn what `vkshot`/`nogeometry`/`hudlog` do (the dev's own screenshot and render-debug switches may save writing our own readback code).
- A GPU readback of the scene target (dump to PNG) is the next tool if the trace shows a plausible scene pass but its output is still black; not written.

### Hypotheses for the black world, ranked by my current guess [ALL INFERRED, none tested]
1. NONE load/store ops mis-handled (v29 rewrites them). 2. Fast-math NaN/Inf in the scene or tone-map shaders (v29 turns fast-math off). 3. Wrong baked or dynamic state for 3D (cull, depth op, front face; VKTRACE shows the dynamic ones). 4. The 3D target is rendered but the composite/tone-map pass reads the wrong image or format (VKTRACE pass list). 5. A format/feature gap (RGBA16F or R32F render-target blending, depth 130 sampled as `nearest`, `depth clip control 0`). Closed by the user: frame generation, graphics quality LOW/MEDIUM.

Tested **off-device, pure logic, x86 Linux**: the YAML parses (15 steps); `bash -n` passes on every run step; the CI strings step's Python was compiled and run on a synthetic string blob (new `P2RE_` entries and widened neighbour window print); the new C block (`p2re_env_setup`, `sh_getenv`) was compiled with gcc `-Wall -Wextra` and AddressSanitizer + UBSan and run against a fake Documents folder (folders `setenv_P2RE_VKFLATPS=1`, `setenv_P2RE_VKSHOT`, `setenv_P2RE_X=hello` applied; `setenv_PATH=evil`, `setenv_P2RE_bad`, `setenv_P2RE_` rejected and logged; `P2RE_VKSHOTDIR` defaulted when `P2RE_VKSHOT` is set; 4 threads x 2,000 `getenv` calls logged exactly 64 distinct names); the new `xref_str.py` (adrp+add search, written into the workflow) was run on a hand-assembled synthetic ELF (both real references found, a decoy on another page rejected). **NOT tested: the Apple/iOS compile of the C block, `llvm-nm` / `llvm-objdump` branches of `xref_str.py` (neither exists in this sandbox), the real library, the device, whether the game imports `getenv` at all (the shim takes effect only if it does), whether any `P2RE_` variable is read through `getenv` (rather than the game's `env` file) and what any of them does.**

2. **`getenv` shim** (`sh_getenv`): answers from the real environment and logs `GUEST getenv("NAME") -> value|(null)` for the first 64 distinct names, so the log shows which switches the game really reads (and at what point).

Output `disasm/xrefs.txt`, `disasm/syms_decl.txt` (symbols that look like FVF / vertex declaration / DrawPrimitive entry points), first lines also in the job log; artifact `p2-disasm`. **This step needs no device run: the build alone produces it.**

## TURN 40 ADDENDUM (the decl check, and a progress estimate)

### Ranges received [CONFIRMED]
`0x59e7730-0x59e8000` (start of `ShadowDrawSkipped` and the head of the real draw function), `0x5a5c030-0x5a5c140` (`NullCurrentFVF`, `NullSurfaceDesc`, `NullSurfaceLevel`, `NullTextureDesc`: the guest-object accessors of the \"null D3D9 device\"), `0x59e2400-0x59e2640` (start of `DeclCreate`: takes a lock via a guard variable, scans a table), `0x59d49e0-0x59d4c30` (start of `Present()`). Note the listing is of the UNTOUCHED ELF (guest base `0x2000000000` still in `orr` immediates).

### The draw function's decl rule (see Turn 40 status for the full text)
Fields of the draw-state snapshot `x26`: `+0` vs id, `+4` ps id, `+8` decl id, `+0x20` pointer used by the fallback `vector::insert` calls, `+0x1a4` FVF. Flow in the head: (a) `0x59e79a4`..: optional filters driven by globals (a name filter against a `strstr` list, time ranges: part of the dev's `drawtrace` / `P2RE_VKSKIPPS` machinery [INFERRED]); (b) vs and ps ids to objects via `DrawStats+0x660` (a helper reached by `bl` into the middle of an unexported function); (c) decl: non-zero id -> `DeclElements` -> continue at `0x59e81bc`; zero id -> FVF -> if `-1` use `NullCurrentFVF()`, if 0 -> missing path `0x59e7e60` -> `0x59e8400` (log path, de-dup, `ngfx::Log`) -> return 0. The code after `0x59e7e0c` (FVF present) builds a decl from the FVF with a cache table (not read yet).
**What this means:** the dropped draws are draws for which the game's own D3D9 state has no vertex declaration and no FVF. A real D3D9 draw cannot work like that, so either (i) those are draws the game issues in a state that the original D3D9 would also have rejected (harmless; also dropped on Android), or (ii) the state snapshot is filled wrongly on this port (our side: guest memory read, an uninitialised field, a shim returning 0), or (iii) the vertex declaration is set through a path the layer reads from a different field. **Cannot be decided without an Android reference or the code that fills `x26`.** The pairs listed in Turn 38 (vs `04000079` with eight pixel shaders etc.) remain the candidates.

### Progress estimate given to the user (Claude's gut feeling, not measured)
About half way to knowing WHERE the picture goes wrong (is the scene target black before the composite, or is the composite wrong), perhaps a third of the way to knowing WHY. The next device runs (`P2RE_VKDEBUGCLEAR`, `rtdump_ps=`) are expected to settle \"where\" in one or two rounds; \"why\" probably needs 2-4 more.

### NEXT (Turn 40 -> 41) [unchanged from Turn 39 NEXT items 2-3, since the user already has the disasm; the IPA run is still needed]
1. If not yet done: run the workflow (new `build-loader-v30.yml`) to get the IPA; install.
2. Device run A: app closed, create `setenv_P2RE_VKDEBUGCLEAR=FF00FF`, start, reach the black world, report the colour of the 3D area (and of loading/pause screens). Send loader log + `p2.log`.
3. Device run B: no setenv folder; with the black world on screen create `rtdump_ps=04000207` (then other ids); send the loader log (`RTDUMP trigger served`, `marker-probe fopen` lines show the real path) and `p2.log` (`rtdump:` range lines).
4. Optional next disasm (only if run B shows the file is never polled or never read): `Present()` body `0x59d4c30-0x59d5100`, and the FVF branch of the draw function `0x59e7e0c-0x59e81bc`, and the code that fills the draw-state snapshot (find callers of the draw function via xref on `0x59e7980`).

### Files received [CONFIRMED]

- The world renders only about 2 times in 5; the other launches are black on black. In build 30 (debug colour magenta) the magenta also appears on the bad launches.
- Reading old configs does not matter; it is luck of the draw.

### Good vs bad launch [CONFIRMED numbers from `p2.log` per-second lines]
| | good | bad |
|---|---|---|
| passes per second | about 1,980 (about 33/frame) | about 60 (about 1/frame) |
| draws per second | about 3,000 | about 620-660 (later 1,300-4,600 during loading) |
| skipped per second | about 120 (all "no shader/decl") | about 2,480-2,560 (all "no shader/decl") |
| `decl 00000000` draw-left-out keys logged | 1 | 32 |
| `compositeAlpha` | 1, forced 1 | 1, forced 1 |
| `TPIDR_EL0` at start | 0x0 | 0x2 (noise; see TL;DR 7) |
Clears per frame are about 10 in both (about 90% folded into load ops); passes only open at draws, so with the scene draws dropped the passes never open. Decl ids seen in "no pipeline" lines: good `05000001` only; bad `05000001`, `05000002`. The same shader pair had decl 0 in the good run (`vs 0f ps 24`, one drop) and `05000001` in the bad run, so even individual draws flip between runs. Decl ids differ between runs for the same shaders (`vs 3b ps 3c`: `05000001` good, `05000002` bad), consistent with creation-order differences (`DeclCreate` dedups by content and uses the creation index).

### Ordering difference at startup [CONFIRMED log order, meaning INFERRED, n=1 pair]
Good `p2.log`: `state 6` (line 95) -> "graphics LOW: the game's shadow detail 1 -> 0 / AO off / motion blur off" (100-102) -> `state 0` (103) -> effect-off shaders (155+). Bad: `state 6` (95) -> `state 0` (102) -> several "effect off" and about 15 `decl 00000000` drops (126-141) -> the override lines (150-152). The strings "graphics LOW: the game's shadow detail" and "ambient occlusion off" are referenced from guest functions `f_10247c90(Cpu*)` at 0x5a0057c and `f_101f9c00(Cpu*)` at 0x5a005f8 (from `xrefs.txt`). Also: the good log shows an extra "first launch default" line and no `controls.cfg`; the user says config state is irrelevant.

### Listing facts [CONFIRMED listings; the symbol labels are nearest-symbol only]
- `DeclCreate(const void*, unsigned)` 0x59e2400: takes a global spinlock at `0x5d92868`; deque at `0x5d8e750` (entries 32 bytes, 128 per block; entry +0 valid byte, +8 `vector<uint8>` of elements); index 0 reserved, lookup from index 1, memcmp dedup; returns `0x05000000 | index`; releases with `stlrb`.
- `DeclElements(unsigned)` 0x59e2b20 and `ShaderInfo(unsigned, ShaderView&)` 0x59e2a00 take the same lock. `ShaderInfo` uses a deque of 40-byte entries, 102 per block, id tag `0x04`.
- `NullCurrentFVF()` 0x5a5c030 only returns the global at `0x5daf798`.
- Callers of `DeclElements`: 0x59e7de4 and 0x59eb474, both inside the draw function (labelled `ShadowDrawSkipped`; it only follows the 0x1.. id range test `id-1 < 0x0fffffff`). **The only caller of `DeclCreate` is `f_109a2950(Cpu*)` at 0x59fcc40 (call at 0x59fccec).**
- `f_109a2950` (the decl hook): reads guest ESP, takes `[esp+4]` as the elements pointer (w20), calls `f_109a2950_lifted` (0x54b42e0), reads EAX (the created object, w19); if both non-zero it scans 8-byte elements for stream `0xFF` (max 64), calls `DeclCreate(host ptr = guest | 0x2000000000 (patched to the window base), count)`, takes lock `0x5daf2d0`, inserts `object -> id` into an `std::unordered_map<uint,uint>` at `0x5daf2d8` (node value at +0x14), calls `0x5b23c30` (probably an atomic add on `lock+0x34`, a generation counter; not dumped), releases with `stlrb`.
- `f_109a85a0` (the shader hook, 0x59fcac0): calls `ShaderCreate(const uint*, uint)`, then the same pattern with lock `0x5daf298`, map at `0x5daf2a0`.
- `0x5b23b10`: outline-atomics swap (see TL;DR 5). `0x5b23740` = cas1, `0x5b23ad0` = cas8; `init_have_lse_atomics` at `0x5b23774` calls `getauxval(16)`, tests bit 8, then `__system_property_get` + `strncmp(…, 10)` against a string at `0x43e326`, and writes the flag byte `0x6039358`. How OUR loader answers `getauxval` / `__system_property_get` was not checked this turn [UNVERIFIED]; either path of the helpers is correct, so this is low priority.

### Reading [ALL INFERRED]
1. In a bad launch the draw snapshot's decl id is 0 for most scene draws because the lookup `object pointer -> decl id` misses (or the decl never went through the hook). The registry itself is not the culprit.
2. The failure is bimodal per launch and persistent for the whole run (about 2,500 drops/s for minutes), so it is a start-up decision (a race that leaves a bad state, or an ordering difference), not a rare per-draw race. A reader that skips the lock while a loader thread inserts/rehashes would only give TRANSIENT misses, so that alone does not fit.
3. Candidate causes, ranked by my guess: (a) decls created through a path other than the hooked function in bad launches (for example an engine call order that differs when the override lands late); (b) the hook skipping registration (`elements == 0` or returned object 0 on a racing path); (c) the reader (SetVertexDeclaration hook) uses a different key than the hook (host vs guest pointer) in some state; (d) unlocked-reader transient misses stacking up (unlikely alone). None tested.

### Not tested / not known
The `SetVertexDeclaration`/`SetFVF` implementations and the reader of the decl map have NOT been seen. Whether the "graphics LOW" ordering difference causes the drops or only accompanies them is unknown (n=1 pair). No v31 loader-side change has been tested against the bug. v31b ran only as CI analysis. v31c has not been run. The Linux/x86 sandbox checks on v31b/v31c were: YAML parse, `py_compile` of the embedded Python, and one synthetic `bl` decode check (pure-logic, off-device); nothing exercised the real library.

### v31b / v31c [WHAT WAS TESTED, exactly]
v31b: same loader build as v31 (header still `== P2 loader v31 ==`); changed only the CI analysis step: `FVF %08x -> ` added to the string xrefs, a scan of every `bl` to `DeclCreate` / `DeclElements` written to `callers.txt`, default `disasm_ranges` = `0x5b23700-0x5b23c00`. Ran in CI (user returned `p2-disasm_4.zip`). v31c: v31b + data-address xrefs (adrp+add) for `0x5daf2d0` ("declmap lock") and `0x5daf298` ("shadermap lock") added as extra targets (limit 14 hits each, 0x200 bytes of context before each), default range blank. Tested off-device: YAML parses; embedded Python compiles. NOT run.

### New facts from `xrefs.txt` [CONFIRMED listings; decode by hand]
1. **Code that references the two map structs** (`adrp`+`add` pairs; the printed function labels are nearest-exported-symbol only):
   - declmap `0x5daf2d0`: `f_109a2950(Cpu*)` at `0x59fccf8` and `0x59fcd10` (the decl creation hook, as known), **`f_109a6240(Cpu*)` at `0x59fd8f8` (NEW: the reader)**, and the static constructor at `0x5a5bdd4`.
   - shadermap `0x5daf298`: `f_109a8570(Cpu*)` at `0x59fc9f8` / `0x59fca10` (**a second shader hook, NEW**), `f_109a85a0(Cpu*)` at `0x59fcb78` / `0x59fcb90` (the known shader hook), **`f_109a6240(Cpu*)` at `0x59fd758` / `0x59fd778` (the reader of the shader ids)**, and the static constructor at `0x5a5bd94`.
2. **The static constructor (`0x5a5bb94` .. about `0x5a5be00`; labelled `__hash_table::__erase_unique` by the nearest-symbol rule, which is wrong)** initialises FIVE registries, each 56 bytes: lock byte `strb wzr,[x22]`; two zeroed 16-byte stores (`+8..+40`, a libc++ `unordered_map<uint,uint>`: buckets ptr `+8`, bucket count `+16`, first node `+24`, size `+32`, max_load_factor `+40` stored from `w23`); two words at `+48` set from the result of a call at `0x5b23c60` (`lsl w8,w0,#28`) and `w24` (meaning UNKNOWN); and registers an `__cxa_atexit` destructor. Addresses: `0x5daf1f0` (496), `0x5daf228` (552), `0x5daf260` (608), **`0x5daf298` (664, shader)**, **`0x5daf2d0` (720, decl)**. The first three are other object kinds (textures/buffers/surfaces is a guess) [INFERRED].
3. **The reader `f_109a6240(Cpu*)` (starts `0x59fd5b0`) [decoded from `0x59fd6f8..0x59fd940` of the dump; its head `0x59fd5b0-0x59fd6f8` and tail after `0x59fd940` were NOT dumped]:**
   - `P = *(x22-348)` (pointer to a "current shader state" struct): `vsobj = [P+0]`, `psobj = [P+4]`.
   - vs: if `vsobj == 0` the id is 0, else `id = lookup(0x5daf298, [vsobj+40])`, stored at argument `+0`. ps: if `psobj == 0` 0, else `lookup(0x5daf298, [psobj+36])`, stored at `+4`. (`lookup` = the unexported helper at `0x59fae80`, called with `x0` = the registry struct and `w1` = the key; it is the thing that locks and does the unordered_map `find`; its body is **UNSEEN**.)
   - samplers: `ShaderSamplerMask(vsid)` and `(psid)` give masks; for each set bit it calls `0x5a15220(guest word)` and stores results at argument `+184 .. +264` (vs 4 words, ps 16 words) [INFERRED: texture/sampler object ids].
   - decl: `Q = *(x22-456)`; `idx = [Q+24]` (signed); **`if (idx < 0) id = 0; else id = lookup(0x5daf2d0, [ [Q+0] + idx*16 + 12 ])`**; stored at argument `+8`. Then copies two 32-byte constant tables into the argument at `+280...`.
   - `x19`/`x0` = the function's `Cpu*` argument. The three ids are written at the same offsets (+0/+4/+8) at which the draw function reads its snapshot `x26` (Turn 40), which is why this is very probably the snapshot filler [INFERRED, not proven; the caller was not read].
4. **What this settles about the Turn 42 question "where can `decl 00000000` come from":** exactly two places: (a) **no decl bound**: `[Q+24] < 0`; (b) **a miss in `lookup(0x5daf2d0, key)`**: `key` = the object pointer stored in a 16-byte entry of an array at `[Q+0]`, index `[Q+24]`. Shader ids use the same helper and DO resolve in the dropped draws (vs `04000079` etc. are non-zero in the log lines), so a broken helper is less likely unless the problem is per-registry (a stuck lock in the decl registry only, or an empty decl map).
5. **Still not seen [UNKNOWN]:** the body of `lookup` at `0x59fae80` (does it wait for the lock or try it once and return 0? what does it return on a miss?); who writes `[Q+24]` and the entries at `[Q+0]` (the SetVertexDeclaration side; `Q` is a guest-memory struct reached from the global at `x22-456`); who calls `f_109a6240`; the engine function the decl hook wraps (`f_109a2950_lifted`, `0x54b42e0`).

### Candidate causes after this turn [ALL INFERRED, none tested]
A. **The decl map is empty or sparse in a bad launch** (the decl hook was bypassed or ran before the registry was usable): `lookup` misses for every key. Test: `MAPSTATE DECLMAP size` in a bad launch (0 or tiny) vs a good launch.
B. **The decl registry lock is stuck at 1** and `lookup` try-locks / gives up: persistent misses for decls only, shaders fine. Test: `MAPSTATE DECLMAP lock=1` persisting; read `lookup` to see whether it can give up.
C. **No decl bound (`[Q+24] < 0`)** at draw time because the engine's state-setter stored the wrong index or the entries were not written (a guest-side ordering difference). `MAPSTATE` would show a populated map with lock 0 in this case, and the next disassembly (`0x59fd5b0-0x59fd6f8`, `0x59fae80...`) plus the state-setter would be needed.
D. **Key mismatch**: hook registers `EAX` (object pointer returned by the lifted original) while the reader keys by `[entry+12]`; they differ in a bad launch (a different decl object than the one registered). `MAPSTATE size` alone will not show this; would need the key lists.
(The startup-ordering observation of Turn 42, n=1 pair, is unchanged and still unexplained.)

## TURN 44 ADDENDUM (v32: sampling the decl binding; what is wrong in the pasted snippet)

### Files received [CONFIRMED]
`handoff-turn43.md`, `build-loader-v31d_orig_yml.txt` (the v31d workflow, 2,956 lines) and `google-ai-findings.txt` (3,138 bytes, text written by another AI about the user's v31d run). **No loader log, `p2.log`, `p2-disasm` zip or screenshot came with it.** The user's only text: "I just need a new build. And a new handoff." (so Claude did the build and this file, with no questions, per §13).

### What the pasted findings claim [SECOND-HAND, not checked by Claude]
1. `MAPSTATE DECLMAP size=25 buckets=47 lock=0` in both a good and a bad launch; `w52` counter 0xd1b (good) vs 0x870d (bad); atomics engine normal. If true: the decl map is populated and unlocked in bad launches, so Turn 43 candidates A and B are out, and C (no decl bound) or D (key mismatch) remain.
2. The reader function and the two ways to get `decl 00000000` (already in Turn 43).
3. The startup race (override after state 6 lands about 40 log lines late in a bad launch) is the Turn 42 observation, n=1 pair, unchanged. The paste's wording "the state layout registers properly" because of the override is an explanation, not evidence.

### Problems in the pasted spy snippet [CONFIRMED by reading it; Claude's reading]
- `gLastFailingKey` is declared and printed but never assigned: it would always print `0x00000000`.
- It uses `printf` in the loader; the loader logs through `L()`.
- It reads one sample per 2 s. `[Q+24]` is the engine's CURRENT bound-decl index; it changes between draws, so one instant says little. A good launch can also show a negative index at some instants.
- It treats the global as a 64-bit host pointer at `gBase + 0x5daf2d0 - 456`. That address arithmetic is only an INFERENCE (see below), and the guest-address alternative (32-bit value, host = `0x7800000000 + value`) was not considered.
- Its proposed cure (force `[Q+24] = 0` when negative) is NOT adopted: index 0 selects entry 0 of the engine's decl array, which is a different (wrong) vertex declaration; that would replace "draw dropped" with "draw with the wrong layout", and it would hide the real cause. A cure only makes sense after we know who writes `[Q+24]`.

### Where the Q global is [INFERRED, not proven]
The reader addresses `x22-348` (current shader state pointer P) and `x22-456` (decl state pointer Q). The shader map is at `0x5daf298` and the decl map at `0x5daf2d0`, 56 bytes apart, and the reader's xrefs to both sit in one function: that fits `x22 = elf+0x5daf2d0` (shader map = `x22-56`). Then Q's global is `elf+0x5daf108` and P's is `elf+0x5daf174`, both in the same .bss area as the five registries. **Not verified**: the instruction that sets `x22` (it is in the head of the reader, `0x59fd5b0-0x59fd6f8`, which IS in v31d's default `disasm_ranges`, so the user's `p2-disasm` zip from the v31d run should show it). Also unknown: whether the global holds a 64-bit host pointer or a 32-bit guest address. **v32 therefore tries both readings and logs which worked (`mode=1` host pointer, `mode=2` guest address, `mode=0` neither).**

### v32 (`build-loader-v32.yml`, patch of the user's v31d) [WHAT WAS TESTED, exactly]
Change: workflow name, header `== P2 loader v32 ==`, and a new `DROPMON` block in `p2core.c`; `mem_thread` now starts each 2-second round with `dm_window()` instead of `sleep(2)` (same period, MEM/ARENA/MAPSTATE lines unchanged). Nothing else changed (all v30/v31 switches, CI analysis steps, default `disasm_ranges` stay as in v31d).
`dm_window()` samples every 5 ms for 2 s (400 samples). Each sample: read the 8 bytes at `elf+0x5daf108` (Q); if the value looks like a host pointer use it, else if it is a 32-bit value use `0x7800000000 + value`; read `idx = (int32)[Q+24]`; if `idx < 0` count it; else read the entries pointer `[Q+0]` (same interpretation), `key = [entries + idx*16 + 12]`, and walk the decl map by hand (map struct at `elf+0x5daf2d0`: first node pointer at +24, size at +32; libc++ node: next at +0, key at +16, value at +20) to see whether `key` is in the map. One line per window (the first 40 windows, then every 5th):
`DROPMON win=N samples=400 qbad=<Q unreadable> idx<0=<n> idx>=0=<n> (hit=<n> miss=<n>) rdfail=<n> idx[min max last] key=0x.. id=0x.. missing_key=0x.. Qraw=0x.. mode=<0|1|2>`.
Tested **off-device, pure logic, x86 Linux** (gcc `-Wall`, AddressSanitizer + UBSan, fake memory with a hand-built decl map of 2 nodes): host-pointer mode and guest-address mode, each with idx 3 and the key in the map (all 400 samples `hit`, id returned), idx 3 and the key absent (all `miss`, `missing_key` shown), idx -1 (all `idx<0`); unreadable memory (`qbad=400`, no crash, no noise). YAML parses (15 steps); `bash -n` clean on every run step. **NOT tested: the Apple/iOS compile of the block (it uses only `safe_rd`, `gBase`, `L`, `usleep`, `memcpy`, `memset`, all already used in the same file), the real library, the device, whether `elf+0x5daf108` really is Q, whether the libc++ node layout (key at +16, value at +20) matches this build (the hook's insert uses node+0x14 for the value, which supports it, but the key offset is assumed), whether `safe_rd` at 200 Hz (about 4 reads per sample) costs frame time (expected negligible).** If the node layout is wrong, `miss` would be wrongly high; the `id=` field and a comparison with the good launch tell: in a good launch `hit` must dominate.

### How to read the next logs [INFERRED plan]
Compare a good and a bad launch, window by window (the first 10-20 windows after the world should be visible).
- `mode=0` or `qbad` = all samples, or `rdfail` large in BOTH launches: the Q address guess is wrong. Do not draw conclusions; read the `x22` setup in `disasm.txt` (range `0x59fd5b0-0x59fd6f8`) and fix the address.
- Bad launch: `idx<0` is most of the samples, good launch: `idx>=0` mostly -> **candidate C**: the engine never binds a decl (or unbinds it). Next: find the writer of `[Q+24]` (SetVertexDeclaration side) and what runs before it in a bad launch.

- Both launches show mostly `hit` and few `idx<0`: the sampler does not see the problem; the drops come from a different state (the draw snapshot is filled elsewhere, or Q is not the state that matters).
Caveat: this samples the CURRENT state at random instants, not the state at each draw; compare fractions between launches, not single windows.

So the decl map, the Q address fix, the lock, and "no decl bound" are not the discriminator; the sampler reads the device-wide bound decl, not the per-draw snapshot, so it cannot see the stale-cache candidate (E) directly either. **Candidates A, B, C are dead; D is unsupported; E is neither proven nor killed.**
2. **Startup order reproduced again (this pair, lines in the logs):** good: `state 6 target 3` (417) -> `graphics LOW` override x3 (421-423) -> `state 0 target 3` (425) -> first `draw left out` (439, ONE line, a UI shader). Bad: `state 6` (419) -> `state 0` (425) -> 30+ `decl 00000000` lines (437-482) -> override (484-486).
3. **NEW [CONFIRMED lines]: the two launches build DIFFERENT vertex buffers at `state 6 target 3`, before the first frame.** Good: 2 VB locks, `stride 16 count 4 size 64` (a 4-vertex quad). Bad: 4 VB locks, `stride 24 count 3 size 72` (3-vertex, 24-byte vertices). The game took a different code path before any draw, which fits "full-screen primitive created one way vs another" depending on whether the override has already run. Whether that is why the bad launch's full-screen draws have decl 0 is INFERRED, untested.
4. **Bad-run symptom shape (same as Turn 42):** ~2,520 skipped/s at 60 passes/s early (1 pass per frame), good ~120 skipped/s at ~1,980 passes/s.
5. **Reading [INFERRED]:** the override ("shadow detail 1->0, AO off, motion blur off") is applied by something that races the state machine. If it lands after `state 0`, scene objects are created in the wrong mode and stay wrong for the whole launch. The override's thread is UNKNOWN (the log carries no thread ids).

**v34 WRITTEN (`build-loader-v34.yml`, v33 + 2 changes in the `alog` shim; YAML parses; the patched `alog` block compiled with gcc and run on x86 with stubs, pure-logic only; Apple compile NOT done, device NOT run):**
(a) every game log line now ends `{t=<thread id> m=<monotonic ms>}`, so the next log shows which thread logs `state 6/0`, the override, and `draw left out`, and the exact timing; (b) folder `order_gate` in `Documents` (off by default): the thread that logs `state 6 target 3` waits up to 1.5 s until the override's last line has been logged, then logs `ORDERGATE: waited N ms ... (seen=0|1) on thread T`. If both lines come from the same thread it just times out (seen=0), harmless. Header `== P2 loader v34 ==`.

### Hypotheses (ranked; all INFERRED)
1. **A thread-order race builds different children in the object tree.** For: the .rcf open order differs between launches; the guest heap diverges before state 6. Against: `ncpu_1` and `hold_s5_1500` did not help (but whether they fired is unverified, the game may not size its thread pool from sysconf, and the hold pauses only one thread).
2. **Cold vs warm pipeline cache changes timing.** The first good launches of the day probably ran with an empty or small cache. Weak: the good run above also loaded 4658 KB. Free test: delete `Documents/vkpddi_cache`, launch 3 times.
3. **The game reads something time-valued (QueryPerformanceCounter, timeGetTime, GetTickCount, GetSystemTime, rand seed) and the tree depends on it.** Untested; those shims are not logged.
4. **The loader's own changes since v31 shifted the odds** (DROPMON 200 Hz sampler, tags, VBCHAIN, SLEEPTRACE). The bad launch already existed at v31d, so not the root cause, but the ratio may have moved.
5. **Uninitialised or reused memory from our allocation shims.** The heap divergence fits; nothing logged yet.

### NEXT STEPS (ordered; combine, do not spend a round per idea)
1. Ask the user for ONE log from a launch made with both folders present; grep it for `ncpu switch:` and `HOLD:` to confirm the switches fired. If they did not fire, fix the switch code and re-test; if they fired, the simple form of hypothesis 1 is dead.

## TURN 54 ADDENDUM: v40 test matrix and decision tree
All switches are empty folders in Documents, made with the app CLOSED. Log lines to look at: `RACEPROBE`, `THREADSTART`, `IMGCHAIN`, `CLOCKPROBE`, `HOLD6`, `SETTLE6`, `PDELAY`, `imgdelay switch`, `present switches`, then the usual `state 6` VB lines (good = `stride 16 count 4`, bad = `stride 24 count 3`).
RUNS (user): (1) BASELINE: no switch folders, launches until one good and one bad are captured (send both logs). (2) `settle_s6`: 4 launches. (3) `imgdelay_3000`: 6 launches (3 ms per image x ~160 = ~0.5 s of delay on whichever thread creates images). (4) `pdelay_400` then `pthrottle_5000` if (3) is inconclusive. (5) `hold_s6_1500` as a control.
WHAT EACH RESULT MEANS:
- H1 (current best): the object tree differs because the texture/image creation (render-side) is finished or not when the state thread reaches state 6. Predictions: BASELINE bad has RACEPROBE `images=~160+` at `state 6`, good has `images=~2`; `settle_s6` => bad ~always (SETTLE6 line shows the wait); `imgdelay_3000` and/or `pdelay_400` / `pthrottle_5000` => good much more often. If so the FIX is an ordering guarantee in the shims: make the state thread wait at state 6 until image creation is quiet is the BAD direction, so the fix is the opposite: hold image creation until the tree has been built (or give the render thread the same head-start the original had on Android); to be built as v41 from the best-performing switch, made permanent.
- H1b: texture count is a symptom of a thread-start-order race (THREADSTART order/timing differs between good and bad). Then compare THREADSTART lists (fn elf offsets, ms spacing) between the good and bad baseline; the fix is to serialize thread start (create each guest thread and wait until it reaches its first blocking point) in `sh_create`.
- H2: no switch changes the odds and RACEPROBE `images` are equal in good and bad => the split is not texture timing. Next: CLOCKPROBE (are any time values different, e.g. a monotonic origin or a first-call value: the game may seed or time-gate on `clock_gettime`), IMGCHAIN (which engine path creates the images, from the chain disassemble the caller), and read `f_108b6830` (0x4f0c0b0) and `f_1072deb0` (0x43a2d00) in p2-disasm.zip (not yet opened).
- H3: the delay folders change odds only for `hold_s6` (state thread) => the race is between the state thread and a thread that is not the image creator; use THREADSTART tids + the VBCHAIN creator tid to find it.
- H4: bad and good differ in guest heap addresses before state 6 (first state-6 VB ptr: good `02293110`, bad `024196f0`): if every probe is equal, the allocator sequence is the diff; add a malloc/free ring log (host `malloc` shim sizes) in v41.
WHAT TO SEND BACK: each run's log (P2 log files with date stamps), and say which switch folders were present. Nothing else.

### The `__cxa_guard_acquire` abort: what is and is not known
- Known [CONFIRMED]: abort message text; identical last two guard frames; two different caller functions (`59fe630`, `59fed5c`, both in the region of the runtime's decl/shader hook code near `0x59fcc40` / `0x59fd5b0` / `0x59fae80`, [INFERRED from address proximity only]); crash occurs in good- and bad-shaped launches and both before and after state 6.
- NOT known: whether the guard being hit is the game's/libc++abi's own function-local static in host code, why a same-thread re-entry happens, whether fibers are involved (theory in Turn 55 item 5), and whether the abort exists on Android.
- Next-step range list is in Turn 55 item 6. If the guard function turns out to compare a thread id, the candidate fix is a loader shim for `__cxa_guard_acquire/release/abort` that keys the in-progress state by fiber (or by guard + current fiber) instead of by host thread; do NOT design it before reading the disassembly.

## TURN 56 ADDENDUM: the guard abort is the `syscall` shim (read from `p2-disasm.zip`)

### Uploads this turn
`handoff-turn55.md`, `p2-disasm.zip` (`disasm.txt`, `callers.txt`, `markers.txt`, `syms_decl.txt`, `xrefs.txt`; only `disasm.txt` is new: ranges `0x5b05d00-0x5b06880`, `0x59fe580-0x59fe640`, `0x59fed00-0x59fed70`). No loader logs, no workflow file this turn, so NO new build was written.

### What the listing shows [CONFIRMED text, decode by hand]
- `__cxa_guard_acquire` (symbol at `0x5b05cdc`, libc++abi, statically linked): the guard is a byte pair: `[g+0]` = done flag, `[g+1]` = state (bit 1 = in progress, bit 2 = someone waits), `[g+4]` = 32-bit id of the thread running the initializer. It takes the global mutex `0x6039070` (`pthread_mutex_lock`), and when the guard is in progress it compares `[g+4]` with the result of **`syscall(178)`**; equal means \"same thread, recursive init\": `bl 0x5b06674` with the string at `0x445e1b`, the return address `0x5b05e04` is the second frame in every crash log. Not equal: `pthread_cond_wait` on `0x6039098`. Free guard: stores `syscall(178)` at `[g+4]`, state = 2. `__cxa_guard_release` (`0x5b05e20`) sets done = 1, state = 1, broadcasts if a waiter bit was set. `__cxa_guard_abort` (`0x5b05ec8`) clears `[g+4]` and the state.
- `0x5b06674` (the abort-message helper): `android_set_abort_message`, `openlog`, **`syslog(2, fmt, x2)`** with `x2` loaded from the helper's own frame (`ldr x2,[x29,#0x18]`), `closelog`, `abort`. The crash frame `5b06770` is the instruction after the `syslog` call.
- Callers: `f_10895b40` (guard `0x5daf560`) and `f_10862230` (guard `0x5daf570`), see the Turn 56 status item 3.

### Reading [INFERRED, strong]
1. Our `syscall` goes to Darwin unchanged (IMPORT RISK since Turn 28; no entry in the shim table that I know of; **`build-loader-v42.yml` was not in this turn's uploads, so check the table there before writing**). Every thread therefore gets the same (or an arbitrary) \"thread id\" from `syscall(178)`.
2. A race: thread A is inside the static initializer (guard in progress, `[g+4]` = A's id); thread B reaches the same guard, reads `[g+4]`, calls `syscall(178)`, gets the same value, aborts. That needs two threads to overlap at the same guard, which is exactly what delays/timing change. It also explains why a good-shaped launch can crash (run 9) and why the crash is rare (about 2 of 10).
3. The garbage SIGSEGV after the abort message is the unshimmed `syslog` reading a variadic argument from the wrong place (the abort would otherwise be a clean `abort()`).
4. Turn 55's fiber theory is dropped for this crash. Guest fibers may still matter elsewhere (no evidence either way).

### What is NOT known
- What Darwin's syscall 178 does here (not looked up); whether `syscall` returns -1 or a stable positive number; whether our `gettid` shim returns a unique id per thread (it should: check); whether other code (outside libc++abi) calls `syscall` for something else (futex 98, `SYS_gettid`, `SYS_tgkill`, `getrandom` 278, ...). The v43 shim should log every distinct number and caller.
- Whether this also causes the good/bad split. **Not claimed.** Thread-id-dependent behaviour elsewhere (libc++ `std::this_thread::get_id`, mutex owner checks, `__thread` init guards) is a candidate to test after the fix.

### v43 design (to be implemented when `build-loader-v42.yml` is re-uploaded; next file name is `build-loader-v43.yml`, ask for the repo list first)
```c
/* Linux aarch64 syscall numbers the guest may use; everything else: log + ENOSYS. */
static long sh_syscall(long n, long a, long b, long c, long d, long e, long f) {
    static _Atomic int logged[512];
    if (n == 178) {                       /* gettid */
        uint64_t t = 0; pthread_threadid_np(NULL, &t); return (long)(t & 0x7fffffff);
    }
    if (n >= 0 && n < 512 && atomic_fetch_add(&logged[n], 1) < 3)
        L("PLAT syscall(%ld) NOT SUPPORTED -> -1 ENOSYS (from elf+0x%lx)", n, caller_elf());
    errno = ENOSYS; return -1;            /* Darwin errno for ENOSYS = 78 */
}
```
(Pure sketch, NOT compiled; use the existing named-register-file/variadic machinery since `syscall` is variadic in C: on Android the arguments are in registers x1..x6, so a plain prototype with named parameters works in the register-file style the loader already uses for `open/fcntl/ioctl`. Match that style, not this prototype, when patching. Check the existing `gettid` shim returns the same kind of id so both agree.) Also: `sh_syslog` (priority, fmt, then register-file args via the existing `bio_scan_slots`/printf rebuild; fallback: log the format string only), `sh_vsyslog` if imported, `sh_openlog/closelog` as no-ops. Add a `SYSCALLTRACE` line for the first 3 calls per number. Keep `imgdelay` default as is for the first test; test matrix: 10 launches no folders (count good/bad/crash), then 10 with `imgdelay_0`.

### Decision tree for the next turn
- v43 + 10 no-folder launches: crash count 0 -> abort fixed; compare good rate with v42 (8/10). Then `imgdelay_0` x10: if good rate rises vs the old 1/15, the thread-id bug was part of the split too.
- If crashes persist with a different message: read the new log's first abort line and frame chain; the guard path is excluded by the shim log (`syscall(178)` calls should show up).
- If good rate unchanged with `imgdelay_0` (about 1/15): the split is a separate race; continue with higher delays (`imgdelay_6000/7000/8000`, 10 launches each) and reading thread-id uses.

Changelog: 2026-11-03 Turn 56: read the guard disassembly; found `syscall(178)` thread-id use and the `syslog` variadic abort path; fiber theory dropped; v43 designed, not written (needs `build-loader-v42.yml`).

## TURN 59 ADDENDUM: v44 = real audio output

Uploads: none new (v43 logs from Turn 58 were reused). `build-loader-v44.yml` patched from my own v43 output (91 changed lines).
- **Design [WRITTEN, not device-tested]:** the game's AAudio data callback is still driven by our `p2-aaudio` thread, but the buffer now goes into a 256 KB single-producer/single-consumer ring; an iOS **RemoteIO audio unit** (AudioToolbox, input scope stream format = what the game asked for: int16 or float32, N channels, rate) drains it from its render callback (underrun -> silence + counter). The hardware pulls, the thread only calls the game callback while the ring holds less than **50 ms** (`AU_TARGET_MS`), so the device clock paces the game (no drift). Callback chunk stays 480 frames. Folder **`audio_off`** restores the old silent behaviour. `p2_audio_session()` (new, in `main.m`) sets `AVAudioSessionCategoryPlayback` (plays with the silent switch on) and activates the session. Link line gained `-framework AudioToolbox -framework AVFoundation`.
- **Format handling:** `AAudioStreamBuilder_setFormat/setChannelCount/setSampleRate` now record and log their values (defaults int16 / 2 ch / 48000; AAudio format 1 = int16, 2 = float from memory of the NDK header, verify against the log line `PLAT AAudio setFormat N`). Formats other than 1 and 2 (24-bit, 32-bit int) are NOT supported: log `AUDIO output NOT started (code -3 ...)` and stay silent. `getSampleRate` returns the recorded rate.
- **Log lines to look for:** `PLAT AAudio setFormat/setChannelCount/setSampleRate`, `AUDIO output started: 48000 Hz, 2 ch, int16|float32, ring target 50 ms` (or `NOT started (code ...)` / `StreamFormat rejected: OSStatus ...`), then every 1500 callbacks `AUDIO callbacks N, ring X bytes, device pulls N, underruns N`. Underruns that keep growing = ring too small/thread starved (raise the target); `device pulls` not growing = the unit is not running.
- **Tested (exactly):** YAML parses; ring push/pull logic compiled and run on x86 with a gated producer, 66 MB through with wraparound, 0 mismatches, 0 underruns (pure-logic, off-device). **NOT tested:** the Apple compile (AudioToolbox calls, `AURenderCallbackStruct`, the ObjC `p2_audio_session`), the audio unit on the device, any sound. The Apple branch is guarded by `defined(__APPLE__) && defined(__arm64__)`, so a CI compile error would be in that block or in `main.m`; paste the red step's last 30 lines back.
- **Known limits / risks [INFERRED]:** about 50 ms plus hardware buffer of latency (aim: lower later); no handling of audio interruptions (phone call, Siri) or route changes (headphones): the unit may need a restart; whether Bink movies (intro videos, currently black and silent) route sound through the same XAudio2 path is unknown; the game's callback now runs at whatever cadence the device pulls (bursts of up to 5 x realtime during the initial prefill), which should be harmless but is untested; stereo mix only if the game really sets 2 channels.
- **Test (user):** launch normally, reach the menu, check for sound; send the loader log (look at the lines above) and say what you hear (music, effects, voices, crackle, lag, pitch too fast/slow = wrong rate). If wrong pitch/noise: the logged format/channel values decide the fix. Next build file: `build-loader-v45.yml`.
- **Futex (`syscall(98)`) decision [user asked, Turn 59]:** parked. It would only reduce spinning/CPU cost; the call rate is unmeasured and the game runs smoothly; it fixes neither audio nor textures.
Changelog: 2026-11-03 Turn 59: audio wiring written (v44).

### Uploads this turn
