### User statements [VERIFIED as user reports]
- v44 audio works, "perfectly in just one step". (Not checked against a log: whether any `AUDIO callbacks N ... underruns N` growth exists is UNKNOWN.)
- Requirement for this build: **ZERO REGRESSIONS**; follow the handoff exactly (the user's words, paraphrased).

### What changed, exactly (diff v44 -> v45 on the user's file)
1. `name:` line: `Build loader v45 (futex call counts; real futex behind folder futex_real)` (standing rule: only what changed, one short line).
2. New block before `sh_syscall`: `fx_note` (lock-free per-command and per-caller counters, 8 caller slots), the futex parking lot (`fx_real`, `fx_bucket`, `FxW`, `FxB`, 64 buckets, `FX_SLICE_NS` = 100 ms), `cpu_report` (adds `#include <sys/resource.h>` mid-file, as the file already does for other headers), `fx_report`. A `CLOCK_UPTIME_RAW` fallback `#define` guards the case where the SDK lacks it.
3. `sh_syscall` rewritten around the same logic: counts, `SYSCALLTRACE` first 3 per number (the `NOT SUPPORTED` text is kept for everything but a futex call in real mode), `178` -> `sh_gettid()`, `98` -> `fx_note` and (real mode only) `fx_real`, everything else `errno = ENOSYS; return -1`.
4. `fx_mode()` defined right after `mk_has` (it needs `gDocs`): reads the folder `futex_real` ONCE, as soon as Documents is known (before that it answers 0 = v44 behaviour), and logs `FUTEX mode: ...` once.
5. `P2_SWITCHES.txt` text gained two lines (`futex_real`, FUTEXTRACE note).
6. Telemetry thread: `fx_report(); cpu_report();` right after the existing `MEM footprint` line (that `if` has no braces; the new statement is a separate statement after it, which is intended).
7. Header `== P2 loader v45 ==`.

### What the new log lines look like (to read the next run)
- `FUTEX mode: off, syscall(98) -> -1 ENOSYS as in v44 (folder futex_real absent; ...)` or `FUTEX mode: REAL wait/wake (folder futex_real present; ...)`.
- `FUTEXTRACE call #1..#12: op=0x.. (cmd N) val=.. uaddr=0x.. (from elf+0x..), real futex off|ON`.
- `FUTEXTRACE last ~2 s: N calls (~N/2/s), T so far, mode ...: WAIT(0)=.. WAKE(1)=.. WAIT_BITSET(9)=.. WAKE_BITSET(10)=.. other=.. callers: [e+X xN] ...` and, in real mode, ` | real: eagain, blocked, timeout, slice-wake, woken, wake-with-no-waiter`. **`slice-wake` growing = the 100 ms safety net fired = a waiter whose wake never came (lost wake or a waiter with no waker). That number should stay near 0.**
- `CPUUSE last 2.0 s: X cores busy (user .., system ..; process total .. s)`.
- Caller offsets in `FUTEXTRACE` are elf offsets (as in SYSCALLTRACE). From Turn 58: the futex callers seen were `0x5aaf0f4`, `0x5a8b10c` (the HostDispatch call site; the same address is the caller of the Sleep `usleep`, so it is probably a tail call from a host wrapper), `0x59ce97c`, `0x59f7dd8`, `0x59f7c94` [CONFIRMED earlier; meanings INFERRED].

### Tested (exactly) [all on the Linux/x86 sandbox, pure logic, off-device]
- YAML parses; step count unchanged (15); the only step that differs from v44 is the one that embeds `p2core.c`.
- The new C, extracted verbatim from the build, compiled with gcc `-Wall -Wextra` against stubs for `L`, `safe_rd`, `mk_has`, `gDocs`, `gBase`, `sh_gettid` and a stub `pthread_cond_timedwait_relative_np` built on `pthread_cond_timedwait`: **clean.**
- Default mode (no folder): `syscall(98)` -> -1 / `errno == ENOSYS`; `178` -> the thread id; other numbers -> -1 / ENOSYS; the mode stays off even if the folder appears later (decided once). 5 checks passed.
- Real mode, 24 checks passed: EAGAIN on value mismatch; EINVAL on unaligned, null, zero bitset; bad pointer -> EINVAL or EFAULT; relative timeout (60 ms) and absolute `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` timeout (50 ms) end with ETIMEDOUT at the right time; an already-past deadline times out at once; with no timeout and no waker the call returns 0 after about 100 ms and counts a slice-wake; wake-with-no-waiter returns 0; 3 waiters, wake 1 then the rest returns 1 then 2; a different address and a non-matching bitset wake nobody, a matching bitset wakes one; REQUEUE keeps ENOSYS; 64 waiters on 64 addresses all woken. **Stress: 100,000 futex ping-pong round trips between two threads in about 350 ms, 0 slice-wakes (no lost wake), repeated 3 times.**
- ThreadSanitizer run of the same tests: only 2 reports, both in the HARNESS: the pre-check `safe_rd` copy of the futex word (done before taking the lock; its value is discarded; the real decision is the atomic load under the bucket lock) races with the other thread's atomic store. No report inside the parking-lot logic itself.
- `CPUUSE` formula checked: 2 busy threads on a 1-core sandbox report 0.99 cores.
- In-context compile comparison: the whole `p2core.c` extracted from v44 and from v45, `gcc -fsyntax-only -Wall` on x86 with a fake `layout.h`: **16 errors in both (all from Apple-only headers/code), and the ONLY new diagnostic in v45 is the implicit declaration of `pthread_cond_timedwait_relative_np` (an Apple-only function, same category as `pthread_threadid_np` which v44 already warns about on Linux).**

### NOT tested
The Apple compile (CI), anything on the device: whether the sync on a real iPhone behaves like the x86 harness; real futex timing effects on rendering, audio, the good/bad texture split or the crash; whether `fx_report`/`cpu_report` cost anything measurable (they run once per ~2 s). The range designator `[0 ... FX_NB - 1] = { PTHREAD_MUTEX_INITIALIZER, NULL }` is a clang/gcc extension and compiled on gcc only; a CI error would be in the futex block (paste the red step's last 30 lines back).

### Facts checked this turn by web search (2026-11-03) [per §13: do not rely on memory]
- Linux futex op numbers: `FUTEX_WAIT` 0, `FUTEX_WAKE` 1, `FUTEX_REQUEUE` 3, `FUTEX_CMP_REQUEUE` 4, `FUTEX_WAKE_OP` 5, `FUTEX_WAIT_BITSET` 9, `FUTEX_WAKE_BITSET` 10, `FUTEX_PRIVATE_FLAG` 128, `FUTEX_CLOCK_REALTIME` 256, `FUTEX_CMD_MASK` = ~(128|256), match-any bitset `0xffffffff` [CONFIRMED on kernel `futex.h` mirrors, Chromium and strace sources; not Bionic's own header]. WAIT's timeout is relative, WAIT_BITSET's is absolute [from the futex man page, from memory of its contents; the build follows this and the tests exercise it, but it was NOT re-read this turn].
- `pthread_cond_timedwait_relative_np(cond, mutex, const timespec*)` exists in Darwin's pthread API including iOS [CONFIRMED by third-party Darwin header bindings and Chromium's Mac/iOS condition variable; NOT read from Apple's own SDK header; the CI compile is the real check]. The build's deployment target is `ios15.0` (workflow clang line), which is why `os_sync_wait_on_address` (newer iOS) was NOT used.

### Design decisions and why [INFERRED unless marked]
- **Why a measurement build and an opt-in, not just "implement it":** the Turn 58 rule was measure first; the call rate was never known; and Turn 55-58 showed that anything that moves thread timing (image delay, thread-id fix) moves the good/bad texture split and the crash rate. A default-on futex would change timing for every launch with no baseline.
- **Why Linux errno numbers in real mode but Darwin ENOSYS in the old path:** the guest reads errno through `__errno` -> Darwin `__error` (shim alias), but it was compiled for Bionic and compares against Linux numbers; libc++'s own futex wait ignores the result. The old path's `errno = ENOSYS` (Darwin 78) is kept byte-for-byte for the zero-regression default.
- **Why the 100 ms slice:** any futex user must tolerate spurious wake-ups, so cutting a long wait into 100 ms pieces cannot break a correct caller, and it bounds the damage of a missing wake to 100 ms of latency instead of a hang (v44 callers never blocked at all).
- **What the callers may be:** libc++ `__libcpp_atomic_wait` (2 s timeout, 32-bit state words, wake via `FUTEX_WAKE_PRIVATE` with 1 or INT_MAX) [from memory of libc++ sources, UNVERIFIED]; and possibly the dev's own Win32-layer waits (the `0x5a8b10c` site is a HostDispatch tail-call site). If the latter use `syscall(98)` with other ops (REQUEUE, WAKE_OP, PI), those still return ENOSYS in both modes.

### Test matrix (user)
1. **Baseline check, no folders:** launch as usual for about 10 minutes including the open city; the build must look and sound like v44 (audio, textures, no crash). Send ONE log. We read: `FUTEX mode: off`, the `FUTEXTRACE` lines (calls per ~2 s, which commands, which callers) and `CPUUSE`.
2. **Only if the call rate is meaningful** (rough guide, not a rule: hundreds per second or more): make an empty folder `futex_real` (app closed), same play, same place for the same time. Send one log. Compare `CPUUSE` (cores busy), per-second frame lines in `p2.log`, `AUDIO ... underruns`, and the user's impression (smoothness, heat, battery % used over the same 10 minutes).
3. **Crash and split watch (needed before default-on):** 10 launches without the folder and 10 with it, count good / bad (blocky map) / crash separately, as before. The no-folder baseline is v43's 0 of 20 crashes and about 19 of 20 good.
4. Any `ANDROID ABORT MESSAGE`, `PLAT syslog`, a hang, or `slice-wake` growing fast in real mode means STOP and send the log; the default stays off.

### Decision tree
- Few futex calls (dozens per second or less): the idea is not worth it; leave `futex_real` off, drop the item, record the number.
- Many calls, real mode clean (0 crashes in 10, same good rate, `CPUUSE` clearly lower, `slice-wake` about 0, audio underruns not rising): v46 makes it default ON with an opt-out folder `futex_off`, and the v45 default path stays reachable.
- Real mode shows hangs, crashes, audio underruns or a worse good/bad split: leave off; use the `FUTEXTRACE` caller list to see which caller expects something else (return value, REQUEUE).

### Process notes
- Built as a minimal patch of the user's v44 upload (not rewritten from memory); the diff is the 7 items above. Standing rules honoured: syscall/syslog shims kept, workflow name says only the change, next file number stated (v46), counts from the user marked VERIFIED, exact test scope stated.
- The user's one-line request carried no explicit choice between "measure" and "implement"; both were delivered with the safe default. If the user wants the real futex default-on immediately, that is a one-line change (invert the folder test in `fx_mode`) but is NOT recommended before the matrix above.

### v45 A/B result [from the pasted analysis; the two logs were not re-read this turn]
- Futex calls, first ~60 s: about 8.8 M (off) vs about 110 k (real). Peak rate about 1.6 M/s vs about 3,500/s. Off: nearly all WAIT from `elf+0x5aaf0f4`, getting instant -1 ENOSYS and retrying (a spin loop); real: that caller fell to a few dozen calls per window.
- Real mode clean: blocked and woken counts match; `slice-wake` 0 in every window (the 100 ms safety net never fired); 2 real timeouts; `other`=0 in both logs.
- CPU: 1.82 cores (off) vs 1.81 (real) averaged over the first 24 windows; windows 1-12 1.26 vs 1.28, 13-24 2.38 vs 2.35; system time only about 0.12-0.2 cores. FPS about 58.4 vs 59.1, worst frame 216 ms vs 239 ms (within noise; runs were 90 s vs 60 s, matched by window index only).
- Reading [INFERRED]: real futex is correct and has no visible downside, but gives no battery or smoothness gain. Not tested: crash rate and good/bad split over 10 launches with real futex (the Turn 60 matrix item 3). The v46 default-on is therefore based on log behaviour, not on the 10-launch count.

### v46 (as described in the pasted text; I did not compile it; the pasted text says YAML parses and the new C passed a syntax check against stub Mach headers)
- Futex real ON by default; empty folder `futex_off` restores v44. `THREADCPU` every ~2 s (top threads: tid, name, guest entry offset, cores; worker pool of ~12 threads summed; header carries thermal state and low-power mode); `THERMAL` line on every state change; `PCPROF` about 100 Hz on the 4 busiest threads (guest vs host vs waiting share, hottest guest `e+XXXX`, hottest host functions, which guest code called into the host); folder `pcprof_off` disables it.
- CI step `Dump the guest symbol table` writes `disasm/guest-syms.txt` into artifact `p2-disasm`.

### The `guest-syms.txt` bug [yml lines 50-56 read; cause INFERRED, not run]
- Step runs `"$OBJ" -t work/libp2re.so | awk '$0 ~ / F / { print $1, $(NF-1), $NF }' | sort`. `-t` reads `.symtab`. The library is stripped (§4: "stripped"; 82,143 exports, 80,665 named `f_<hexaddr>(Cpu*)`, 64 `_lifted`, 1,478 others). No `.symtab` means no output; `|| true` and `continue-on-error: true` hide it; the user got an empty 0-byte file.
- Intended content: one line per function, `address size name` (hex), sorted by address, about 80 k lines.
- Fix for v47 (UNTESTED; this sandbox has no llvm tools this turn): use the dynamic table through `llvm-nm`, which the same yml already locates with `xcrun -f llvm-nm` (`xref_str.py`): `NM="$(xcrun -f llvm-nm 2>/dev/null || command -v llvm-nm)"; "$NM" -D --defined-only -S -n work/libp2re.so | awk 'NF==4 {print $1, $2, $4}' > disasm/guest-syms.txt`. Print `wc -l` and the first 5 lines in the job log, and drop `continue-on-error` or fail loudly on an empty file.
- Limit: the `f_<hexaddr>` names carry no meaning, so the file gives function START and SIZE (so offsets map to a function and its size) and meaningful names only for the ~1,478 other exports (`HostDispatch`, `FrameGenOn`, `AdpfRegisterThread`, ...). Naming the game logic needs more (string xrefs, call graph).

### What is needed to pick the performance target
1. v46 loader log from a warm-phone flight in the dense open-world area (a few minutes): at least the `THREADCPU`, `THERMAL`, `PCPROF`, `MEM` lines. 2. A note of when the phone felt hot or frames dropped. 3. `guest-syms.txt` is optional for the first read and can come from any run of the same 1.0.3 library (SHA pinned in the workflow).
Questions to be answered by that log: one dominant thread (render, game logic, worker pool)? time in guest code or host (MoltenVK, system)? does the thermal state step up while CPU stays high? These decide: reduce work (e.g. draw calls per frame) vs something outside the guest code.
